#2797. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for steady income

Stock markets remain volatile as investors weigh uncertainty in the Middle East, rising interest rates and concerns about the artificial intelligence (AI) trade.

Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking a stable income stream can enhance their portfolios with the addition of dividend stocks. In this regard, tracking recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks that are backed by strong financials.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is the first dividend pick. Earlier this month, the company paid a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share. At an annualized dividend of $7.12 per share, CVX offers a dividend yield of about 3.5%.

After a meeting with senior leadership, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on CVX and raised his price target to $240 from $225. Among the key takeaways Mehta emphasized was that Chevron is building a solid pipeline of promising international exploration projects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa and the Eastern Mediterranean. In particular, he mentioned the focus on Venezuela, where Chevron expects to more than double gross production to 600 kbd from about 280 kbd by 2031, using three joint ventures.

Mehta also noted Chevron's efforts to deploy new technologies, especially in shale and tight-oil operations. CVX is focused on boosting productivity and efficiency to maximize free cash flow, he added.

Initiatives such as artificial lift optimization, AI and machine learning, and advanced chemical treatments are expected to improve recovery by about 10% in new wells. Consequently, capital spending per barrel in U.S. shale is projected to fall 25% in 2026, with total Permian spending dropping below $3.5 billion.

Mehta also said that Chevron sees power as a growth opportunity and is using its U.S. natural gas portfolio, key equipment and partnerships to meet rising demand. Notably, Chevron recently signed a 20-year power purchase deal with Microsoft (MSFT) to supply 2.67 GW of behind-the-meter capacity. This project, called Kilby, is expected to see its first power delivery in 2028 and generate mid-teens returns and long-term cash flows.

Mehta ranks No. 536 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 11.3%. See Chevron Statistics on TipRanks.

Enterprise Products Partners (EPD) is a master limited partnership (MLP) that provides midstream energy services to producers and consumers of natural gas, natural gas liquids (NGLs), crude oil, refined products and petrochemicals. With a quarterly cash distribution of 56 cents per common unit ($2.24 per common unit annualized), EPD offers a yield of about 6%.

Following a recent call with the company, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on EPD with a price target of $42. The analyst slightly lowered her estimates for the second half of 2026, citing margin and volume normalization, though demand dynamics remain strong.

While the 5-star analyst expects a sequential decline due to normal seasonality and normalization following high spreads in the first half of the year, she still expects EPD to report another solid quarter (Q3 2026) and enter 2027 with a favorable setup. Ahead of Q3 earnings, Scotto expects investors to focus on export demand durability, EPD's ability to profit from spot cargo sales and growth in Permian volumes.

Scotto continues to expect $150 million of common unit buybacks per quarter in the second half of 2026. She expects buybacks to increase to $200 million per quarter in 2027, as capex spending declines and free cash flow improves.

In total, Scotto is bullish on EPD and views it as a core MLP holding that has both offensive and defensive characteristics. The analyst praised EPD's diverse asset base as ensuring stable cash flows. "Furthermore, the partnership's multi-year organic growth backlog helps provide visibility on long-term distribution growth," Scotto said.

Scotto ranks No. 199 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 15.9%. See Enterprise Products Partners Financials on TipRanks.

Brookfield Infrastructure Partners (BIP) operates a diversified portfolio of high-quality utilities, transport, midstream and data assets. The company declared a quarterly distribution of 45.5 cents per unit, payable on September 29. At an annualized distribution of $1.82, BIP offers a yield of 5.2%.

Recently, BMO Capital analyst Devin Dodge reaffirmed a buy rating on Brookfield Infrastructure with a price target of $47. The analyst cited improved visibility into BIP's growth and views its corporate simplification as a potential catalyst.

"Layering in the benefits from the proposed corporate simplification, a multiple well below historical levels and an attractive yield, we believe BIP offers a compelling risk/reward and it remains a preferred idea," said Dodge.

With more organic growth projects in the pipeline and new partnership investment opportunities, Dodge believes that BIP has clearer visibility into where it will deploy capital. That should support continued double-digit growth in funds from operations (FFO) per unit.

Dodge said the semiconductor foundries that BIP is developing with Intel (INTC) are expected to be fully commissioned by the end of 2026, with returns improving through Q4 2026. Dodge expects the joint venture to boost FFO by 300-400 basis points.

Dodge ranks No. 476 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 68% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.5%. See Brookfield Infrastructure Partners KPIs on TipRanks.

#2796. Weekly Recap 2: Hawks take a step back

We are just shy of the season being a month old, the Atlanta Hawks find themselves as the number four team in the Eastern Conference at 8-6. Despite being one of the better teams in the conference, this past week may have been a step back for a team that found themselves as winners of four straight.

The week's first matchup was against the Los Angeles Lakers at home. The Lakers were winners of five straight heading into Monday's game in Atlanta and were led by Luka Doncic, who was averaging more than 40 points per game at the time. The Hawks' roster is a near ideal group to slow down Doncic's historic pace with multiple versatile defenders on the perimeter. Dyson Daniels drew the assignment of Doncic, which was not a surprise given his reputation as one of the best on-ball defenders in the league.

While Daniels began the night containing Doncic from one of his signature 20-point first quarters, the perennial All-NBA guard displayed his other-worldly passing ability, as he racked up five assists during the opening period, which opened up the Lakers' offense and led them to an early nine-point lead which they did not relinquish for the rest of the game.

Unfortunately for the Hawks, they were plagued by turnovers and poor interior play. Lakers' center Walker Kessler was a matchup nightmare for the Lakers, as he shot 8-for-12 from the field on his way to 21 points and ten rebounds. Kessler's contributions were a significant factor in the Hawks surrendering 52 points in the paint. The poor performance down low has become not just a trend, but a full-blown habit for the Hawks, as they currently allow the third most points in the paint in the entire league.

The Hawks also turned the ball over 20 times, which the Lakers turned into 29 points, as opposed to the Hawks turning their forced turnovers into 14 points. Despite these discrepancies, the Hawks were still in the game late, trailing by only two with just under seven minutes to go. Dyson Daniels was making life tough for Luka Doncic and anyone else he guarded, as he picked up four steals along the way, which has become a signature for the league's leading pickpocket thief.

Even with his strong performance defensively, Doncic proved to be too much for the Hawks down the stretch, as he waved off multiple screens while being guarded by Daniels, which then turned into back-breaking buckets.

The Hawks' two leading scorers, Jalen Johnson and 'NAW' struggled mightily from the field, combining for 12-for-33 shooting, which just simply put is not good enough to beat most teams, let alone a squad that is clicking on all cylinders like the Lakers. Even with CJ McCollum and Aaron Wiggins having big nights off the benches, the poor outing from Johnson and 'NAW' was too much to overcome.

Luckily, the Hawks got their toughest test of the week out of the way with the Lakers. Their remaining two opponents, the Chicago Bulls and Milwaukee Bucks each only had a single win apiece. Smooth sailing, right?

The first matchup of the two was against the Bulls, who would be without their phenom rookie, Caleb Wilson. What should have been a sure-fire win against an undermanned Bulls team turned into a frigid display of three-point shooting from the Hawks that made winning quite difficult.

Frigid may be an understatement for a night that featured them shooting 8-for-40 from behind the arc, which equaled an abysmal 20%. The poor shooting especially plagued them in the overtime period, shooting 0-for-6 and totally stifling their offense.

The biggest star for the Bulls was the newly-acquired Norman Powell, who tallied a season-high 33 points on an impressive 12-for-19 shooting. Josh Giddey also had a triple-double, which included a staggering 14 assists.

On the Hawks side, Jalen Johnson also had a triple-double, which was his first of the season. Despite notching another triple-double under his belt, the feat was outweighed by his poor 11-for-26 shooting night along with five turnovers. CJ McCollum had another productive night off the bench with 20 points and Onyeka Okongwu also chipped in 17 points.

However, these contributions couldn't overcome an unprecedentedly bad shooting night for the Hawks, as they outperformed the Bulls in major categories such as forced turnovers, points in the paint, and fastbreak points.

The week concluded against the Bucks whose poor season has made them perhaps the runaway choice as the worst team in the entire league. Despite their woes of the season so far, they seemed to be the Hawks' second consecutive upset, as the Bucks raced out to 37-18 at the end of the first quarter. The deficit was attributed to red-hot deep shooting from the Bucks and a continued drought from behind the arc from the Hawks.

Luckily for the Hawks, the Bucks did what they do best- get in their own way. The teams essentially traded the shooting trends in the first quarter in the following period, as the Hawks claimed the lead headed into halftime. While the Hawks quickly raced out to a double-digit lead in the third, their shooting struggles returned, as they shot 28% from three in the second half.

Both teams had a sluggish second half, as the periods were plagued by bad shots, sloppy passes, and defensive lapses, which looked more like a bad day of pick-up ball at your local rec center instead of an NBA game. Luckily for the Hawks, the Bucks' worst day is probably the worst day for any team in the league.

Despite late surges from the likes of Gary Trent Jr., whose most recent contract was so inconceivable that it summoned an investigation from the league, the Hawks held on for the win with final score at 113-106.

Luckily for the Hawks, 'NAW' cleaned up his shooting struggles, leading the way with 20 points on 7-for-15 shooting. CJ McCollum also put up 20 points, which included shooting a perfect 9-for-9 from the free throw line and some clutch buckets down the stretch as the game neared its end.

Despite the win, the continued lackadaisical passing, poor shooting, and poor rebounding from the game are trends that look to make the rest of the season a struggle for the Hawks. As I mentioned last week, this long season will not be won or lost in November.

However, poor habits can compound quickly and send teams on a downhill slope that could be tough to recover from. With both the Oklahoma City Thunder and Detroit Pistons both on the docket this week, the margin for error will be much smaller than the competition they faced last week at the hands of the Bulls and Bucks.

#2795. Seahawks Get Glowing Review From Hollywood A-Lister on Mike Macdonald

NEW YORK, NEW YORK - OCTOBER 20: Chris Pratt attends Prime Video's "The Terminal List" Season 2 New York Premiere at Metrograph on October 20, 2026 in New York City. (Photo by Mike Coppola/Getty Images) Getty

SEATTLE, WASHINGTON - NOVEMBER 08: Head coach Mike MacDonald of the Seattle Seahawks and Gardner Minshew #15 of the Arizona Cardinals shake hands after Seattle's 17-3 win at Lumen Field on November 08, 2026 in Seattle, Washington. (Photo by Olivia Vanni/Getty Images) Getty

The Seattle Seahawks have garnered praise from one of Hollywood's biggest names.

Actor Chris Pratt, known for his roles in "Guardians of the Galaxy" and "Jurassic World," hailed head coach Mike Macdonald during a November 17 appearance on "The Pat McAfee Show."

"The Seattle Seahawks are an awesome organization," Pratt said, according to a clip shared by the show's official X account. "What a cool culture and I love what Mike Macdonald is doing."

Pratt, a longtime Seahawks fan, has watched Macdonald build a winning culture in Seattle. His comments come as the team enters its Week 11 bye with a 6-4 record and two straight victories.

Seattle defeated the Las Vegas Raiders 21-13 on November 15, leaning on its defense and backup quarterback Drew Lock to secure the road win.

Pratt's praise adds another voice to the growing support for Macdonald's approach.

GettyNEW YORK, NEW YORK – OCTOBER 20: Chris Pratt attends Prime Video's "The Terminal List" Season 2 New York Premiere at Metrograph on October 20, 2026 in New York City. (Photo by Mike Coppola/Getty Images)

Pratt's connection to Seattle runs deeper than a passing interest in football.

The Hollywood star has publicly supported the Seahawks for years and regularly speaks about his favorite NFL franchise.

During a January 2026 appearance on "Jimmy Kimmel Live!", Pratt discussed his Seahawks fandom, getting fans excited at a game and encouraging his children to support the team.

His recent comments focused on the organization itself.

Macdonald took over for Pete Carroll in 2024, inheriting a franchise with an established identity. He has since put his own stamp on the team while preserving its longstanding principles.

In a February interview published by Seahawks.com, Macdonald described how Seattle's culture has developed under his leadership.

"Our spirit is unique to us and its evolved and changed since we took over but it's still rooted in the foundation of the Seahawks," Macdonald said.

GettySEATTLE, WASHINGTON – NOVEMBER 08: Head coach Mike MacDonald of the Seattle Seahawks and Gardner Minshew #15 of the Arizona Cardinals shake hands after Seattle's 17-3 win at Lumen Field on November 08, 2026 in Seattle, Washington. (Photo by Olivia Vanni/Getty Images)

Seattle enters its bye week following back-to-back wins over the Arizona Cardinals and Raiders.

The defense allowed just three points against Arizona before holding Las Vegas to 163 total yards.

Against the Raiders, Seattle registered six sacks and surrendered only 27 yards in the second half, according to Seahawks.com.

The offense also came through despite the absence of injured starter Sam Darnold.

Lock completed 14 of 17 passes for 240 yards and two touchdowns against Las Vegas. Wide receiver Jaxon Smith-Njigba finished with 171 receiving yards.

Macdonald now has time to address the team's injuries before Seattle visits the San Francisco 49ers on November 29.

The Seahawks still have work ahead in a competitive NFC West. But Pratt's message reflects the confidence Macdonald has inspired in one of the franchise's most recognizable supporters.

#2794. Investor Steve Eisman says the AI neocloud stocks are in 'big trouble' from Nvidia fallout

Investor Steve Eisman said the so-called AI neocloud stocks will be the biggest losers from the unfolding Nvidia scandal.

Nvidia was essentially a "virtual vertical holding company and it's unwinding," said Eisman of "Big Short" fame on CNBC's "Squawk Box." "I think neoclouds are all in big trouble though because they are part of that vertical holding company and I think they're about to get ejected."

Neoclouds, which used borrowed funds to build data centers, have been among the hardest-hit stocks during the recent pullback in the artificial intelligence trade. CoreWeave and Nebius are each down more than 50% the last three months.

All eyes on Wall Street are on chipmaker Nvidia as it reports earnings on Tuesday after the bell while grappling with a potential Chinese espionage scandal. Founder and CEO Jensen Huang resigned on Thursday under a cloud of controversary revolving around his affair with a suspected Chinese spy. The Commerce Department has subsequently moved to close a loophole on China's remote use of data centers.

Investors are worried as scrutiny increases on Nvidia's China revenue, it may pull back on its extensive financing that supported the AI industry.

Frontier algorithm company Anthropic is expected to make its public debut on Thursday as concerns about the company's profitability potential linger amid the rapid and ongoing commodification of AI.

Eisman said he doesn't think the much-ballyhooed offering is very important anymore to the AI trade because expectations have come down.

"I don't think anybody really cares," the investor said.

Instead, Eisman is focusing on how much Democrats will restrict data center building when they take control of Congress and various governorships in January.

"I think the biggest issues by far are how much are, how much are local and state governments going to slow AI data center construction? Is it going to be 100% moratorium? That would obviously be — that would put the country into recession overnight. Or is it something like 10% to 30%?" he said.

In the event of a 10% to 30% slowdown in data center construction, Eisman said the group of companies within the AI trade that he favors the most are the hyperscalers.

"The group that makes out the best are the hyperscalers because they go cash flow positive again," he said.

Correction: Nvidia founder and CEO Jensen Huang resigned on Thursday. An earlier version misstated the day.

#2792. Ranking 8 potential trade targets for the Phoenix Suns

If you're a Suns fan, you've probably noticed all the trade rumors surrounding the team since their disastrous 1-5 start to the season. You also probably noticed that they're getting absolutely annihilated by the playoff-caliber teams that they play, most recently the Lakers and the Spurs.

We knew going into the season that the team lacked a viable point guard other than Collin Gillespie, and that the only true power forward on the team was Rasheer Fleming. Analysts point out that rebounding and three-point shooting were also likely to be an issue. The hope was that someone would step up and fill those roles.

Unfortunately, the guys that needed to step up and address those weaknesses have not, and probably cannot. Jalen Green has been a great teammate and obviously been trying to buy into Coach Ott's system, but it's obvious to all observers he's not able to fill the role of a point guard and is a terrible fit next to Booker. Collin Gillespie had a miserable start to the season, shooting only 34.1% from the field through the team's first 11 games. Rasheer Fleming has been exiled to the deep bench while looking slow and lost when he does play. Miles Bridges is a good rebounder…for a small forward…but struggles against the taller players he's expected to compete with on the boards.

There have been unconfirmed rumors of disgruntlement in the locker room over the lack of a point guard. When Dejounte Murray made a trade demand, rumors immediately swirled that the Suns were interested. Even John "The Trade Reaper" Gambodoro has admitted the Suns are likely going to be looking for defensive rebounders starting in late January.

Complicating matters for most rumors, though, is that the Suns do not have many assets to move. Most of their future draft picks have already been traded away until the early 20230's (ouch). Only a few players on the roster are trade-eligible now: chief among them Jalen Green, Ryan Dunn, Khaman Maluach, Koa Peat, and Miles Bridges. Mark Williams won't be trade-eligible until January 15, 2027. Dunn is playing exceptionally well, and Bridges is doing better than Brooks, Booker, and Green as a starter (also ouch), and trading him now would be a loss since they gave up an unprotected first for him.

So, all the Suns really have to offer, or are ready to offer, is Jalen Green, and he's an extremely distressed asset. Meaning whomever the Suns are likely to get back is also likely to be playing poorly on a big salary. Despite this, the list of rumored trade targets is long. I'll be taking a look at each: why the Suns would want them, the red flags, what it would take to get them at a minimum, and the overall plausibility that they land in Phoenix.

Or, put another way, plausibility is what the odds are that the Suns could put together an offer that another team would accept, and that other teams likely wouldn't outbid. It also takes into account whether the Suns would WANT to do it. Holden Sherman covered three players recently (Dejounte Murray, Tyler Herro, and Jrue Holiday). To spare you all redundancy, I'm only covering Murray (because he's the hot rumor du jour); the other two are too remote a possibility in my mind to be worth it.

Why The Suns Would Do This: Turner is a PF/C who anchored the Pacers' defense through a thrilling Finals run. He can stretch the floor, moves very well in space, and is switchable on defense. The spacing he provides would allow him to play alongside Ighodaro.

NEW YORK, NEW YORK – NOVEMBER 10: Myles Turner #3 of the Milwaukee Bucks looks to pass during the second quarter of the game against the New York Knicks at Madison Square Garden on November 10, 2026 in New York City. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Dustin Satloff/Getty Images) | Getty Images

Red Flags: 30 years old already. Turner has been God-awful the past couple of years with the Milwaukee Bucks. Terrible rebounder for a 6'11", 250-pound player, and he looks out of shape, so he really doesn't help with that problem. Lots of questions about whether he's already cooked.

What it Would Take: Jalen Green for Turner and Ouasmane Dieng as salary filler and maybe a pick or two coming back to Phoenix.

Plausibility: Highly plausible if either Oso or Man-Man get hurt and will miss significant time before Mark Williams comes back. Otherwise, possible but not likely.

Why the Suns Would Do This: The Suns obviously need a ball handler to play alongside Booker. Years ago, Murray once put up 8.8 assists per game, so he used to be capable of running the point, and there aren't a lot of guys on the market that can claim that much.

Nov 9, 2026; San Francisco, California, USA; New Orleans Pelicans guard Dejounte Murray (5) controls the ball against Golden State Warriors guard De'Anthony Melton (8) during the second quarter at Chase Center. Mandatory Credit: Kelley L Cox-Imagn Images | IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Red Flags: Hoo-boy, are there a lot of these. Declining performance, Achilles tear in 2025, age, low shooting percentage, reputation for being a locker room cancer, off-putting social media behavior, and rumors that he is straight up cray-cray based on what may be a burner profile on social media.

What it Would Take: Green for Murray works straight up.

Plausibility: Almost nil unless something changes. Gambo says no interest. Suns will likely try moving Gillespie into the starting unit before resorting to this. The equation changes if New Orleans is desperate enough to dangle a first-round pick or two in front of the Suns to make their headache go away.

Why the Suns Would Do This: Reid is an earth-moving, wide-bodied PF/C who rebounds and shoots threes while averaging 17 ppg and 8.5 rpg. He's only 27 years old and would seemingly mesh well with the current roster construction, including the ability to play alongside Ighodaro.

PHOENIX, ARIZONA – NOVEMBER 12: Naz Reid #0 of the Charlotte Hornets drives to the basket against Jordan Goodwin #23 of the Phoenix Suns during the first half of the NBA game at Mortgage Matchup Center on November 12, 2026 in Phoenix, Arizona. The Suns defeated the Hornets 101-96. (Photo by Kelsey Grant/Getty Images) | Getty Images

Red Flags: Nothing wrong with Reid, but are the Suns really going to try to trade with the Hornets again?

What it Would Take: Reid for Green works straight up despite the salary differential due to Charlotte being below the cap threshold.

Plausibility: Low. Why would Charlotte do this unless they have something else coming back in the form of picks? Also, is the plan for Charlotte and the Suns to just keep swapping until they have each other's rosters?

Why the Suns Would Do It: Sabonis looks like a tremendous fit as a PF/C who is an elite rebounder and league-average three-point shooter, meaning you have to at least pay token respect to the danger of leaving him completely alone outside the arc.

SACRAMENTO, CA – NOVEMBER 13: Domantas Sabonis #11 of the Sacramento Kings dribbles the ball during the game against the Portland Trail Blazers during the 2026 Emirates NBA Cup on November 13, 2026 at Golden 1 Center in Sacramento, California. Red Flags: Sabonis is a sub-par (but not awful) defender and an average-at-best scorer, but he's still better than either Maluach or Ighodaro. The biggest problems are his salary and what the Kings want back for him.

What it Would Take: Mark Williams and Jalen Green for Sabonis meet the salary requirements, but the Kings would likely want young guys (Maluach, Dunn, Peat) and/or draft picks.

Plausibility: Very low but not completely impossible. Gambo says the Suns aren't interested because of Sabonis's high salary, and the Kings are almost certainly looking for draft capital (that the Suns don't have) in return for a guy that has an elite skill coveted by contending teams. In other words, other teams can easily put together a more enticing package.

Why the Suns Would Do It: Randle is a legit 6'9", 250-pound power forward who can shoot the three a little bit, rebound, and pass the ball (5.0 apg). He's a bulldozer on the inside with a nasty streak. He'd be a great fit in a lot of ways given positional need and the fact that he does a lot of things the Suns lack.

NEW YORK, NEW YORK – NOVEMBER 16: Julius Randle #30 of the Brooklyn Nets controls the ball as Anfernee Simons #5 of the Philadelphia 76ers defends during the second half at Barclays Center on November 16, 2026 in the Brooklyn borough of New York City. Red Flags: Randle is almost 32 years old. His production has been declining for several years, particularly his rebounding, which is down to 6.5 per game. That said, it would probably come back up a lot on a team where no one else seems capable of grabbing the ball. He's also considered somewhat "selfish" as a player.

What it Would Take: Green for Randle would work straight up.

Plausibility: Low. Minnesota gave him away for basically nothing last year, which should tell you something. The Suns chose Miles-freaking-Bridges at a higher price than Randle, which should tell you something about the Suns' level of interest. The Nets would probably also like something back in addition to Jalen Green, but both players' value is dropping by the day.

Why the Suns Would Do It: Giddey is a top-tier passer and rebounder for a guard at 6'7" and 214 pounds. He's 24 with career averages of 14.5 ppg, 7.5 rpg, and 6.6 apg. Very good court vision with the size to exploit it as a PG. He's averaging 8.5 apg this year. He checks the blocks to help the Suns as a distributor who can initiate offense and improve the team's defensive rebounding. Potential to be a triple-double guy.

MINNEAPOLIS, MN – NOVEMBER 14: Josh Giddey #3 of the Chicago Bulls dribbles the ball during the game against the Minnesota Timberwolves on November 14, 2026 at Target Center in Minneapolis, Minnesota. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this Photograph, user is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. Mandatory Copyright Notice: Copyright 2026 NBAE (Photo by David Sherman/NBAE via Getty Images) | NBAE via Getty Images

Red Flags: Giddey is a "meh" three-point shooter and defender. He can't really create his own shot, which would hamper his ability to pull teams away from Booker as a second option.

What it Would Take: Jalen Green for Giddey and Zach Collins as filler works. Given Chicago would likely want some sort of draft or young player compensation (Peat, Fleming, Dunn) in exchange, Giddey is clearly the more valuable player right now.

Plausibility: Doable, if the Suns were to put a pick or a young prospect (Fleming?) on the table. Mostly depends on whether Chicago is ready to move on from him, which has been rumored for a while.

Why the Suns Would Do It: Uhh, who wouldn't want Darius Garland? 26 years old, 2-time All-Star. He'll give you 20 ppg and 7 apg night in, night out. Good three-point shooter and a "classic" point guard who would slot in next to Booker seamlessly.

Red Flags: He's 6-1 and an average defender. But other than that, his game is solid, and there's nothing to suggest he's a problem in the clubhouse.

What it Would Take: Jalen Green and Williams plus draft capital, or Jalen Green and Khaman Maluach.

Plausibility: Roughly the same as me marrying Kim Kardashian. Which is to say, zero. If the Clippers decide to move him, the price tag is far more than the Suns can pay, or would be willing to. Again. Other teams can easily put together more attractive trade packages than the Suns for a high-demand asset.

Why the Suns Would Do It: Even at 33, the 6'10", 253-pound PF/C is still a potent defender and well above average player on offense. He's putting down 25 ppg and 10 rpg right now for the woeful Wizards. The Suns have lacked anyone remotely like this since the heyday of Amare Stoudemire. Deandre Ayton (of all people) had his best seasons with Booker and CP3: imagine how Davis would look with Book (and vice versa).

Red Flags: Anthony "Street Clothes" Davis is 33 years, 8 months old and played a grand total of 71 games over the preceding two seasons. He's got a huge contract, is on the decline, and will be looking for another maximum payday.

What it Would Take:  Jalen Green and Miles Bridges or Jalen Green, Khaman Maluach, and Mark Williams

Plausibility: Very close to zero. If Ishbia thought that the younger, healthier Sabonis was too expensive, I can't imagine much interest in Davis. Plus, going after an aging superstar smells too much like the KD and Beal trades.

Most of these trades aren't plausible. Either the price is too high, they're too expensive for Ishbia (per Gambo), or the Suns wouldn't want the player for various reasons (Murray and Turner come to mind). #2791. Dodgers protect four prospects from Rule 5 draft, including top pitcher snatched from Yankees

There might not be a Rule 5 draft in Major League Baseball this winter, with an upcoming lockout threatening to cancel the annual event.

But the Dodgers didn't take any chances on Tuesday, still electing to protect four minor-league prospects who would've been Rule 5 eligible.

In a series of roster moves Tuesday, the Dodgers added pitchers Christian Zazueta, Jackson Ferris, Adam Serwinoski and Luke Fox to their 40-man roster, while designating for assignment pitcher Jake Cousins and recent waiver claim infielder Christian Cairo.

The Dodgers' reigning Minor League Pitcher of the Year Christian Zazueta is continuing his rise through the franchise's minor-league system. MLB Photos via Getty Images

The moves ensure none of the four young pitchers will be subject to a possible Rule 5 draft — in which non-40-man players who have accrued a certain amount of time in their organizations become available for other teams to "draft" and add to their own roster — while also subjecting Cousins (a veteran reliever recovering from Tommy John surgery) and Cairo (who was claimed from the Mets last week) to waivers if they are to remain in the organization.

The last time MLB experienced an offseason lockout, during the 2021-22 offseason, the Rule 5 draft (which is usually held during the winter meetings in December) was canceled and not rescheduled.

It's possible that could be the case again this year, with the league's current CBA set to expire on Dec. 1. But last week, general manager Brandon Gomes said the club would operate as if things were "business as usual," leading the Dodgers to protect their four prospects ahead of a Tuesday afternoon deadline.

Brandon Gomes, general manager of the Los Angeles Dodgers, speaks with media during the MLB general manager meetings in Las Vegas, Tuesday, Nov. 10, 2026. AP Photo/John Locher

The most obvious choice was Zazueta, a 22-year-old right-handed starter whom the Dodgers acquired for reliever Caleb Ferguson from the Yankees three years ago in what has become a steal of a transaction.

A little-known talent at that time, Zazueta has since blossomed into one of the most promising young arms in baseball. He is ranked as the top pitcher (and fifth overall player) in the Dodgers' farm system. He is the No. 33 overall prospect in the sport, according to MLB Pipeline. And he is coming off a strong 2026 campaign in which he posted a 3.44 ERA in his first stint in Double-A and won the club's Minor League Pitcher of the Year award for a second straight season.

Serwinoski and Ferris, two other trade acquisitions in recent years, were also added to the 40-man roster as expected.

Ferris has been on a bit of a roller coaster since joining the organization in the Michael Busch trade from the Cubs, along with since-traded outfielder Zyhir Hope (who was a key part of the deadline blockbuster for Tarik Skubal this past summer). The 22-year-old had impressive stints in Double-A in 2024 and 2025, but then saw his ERA balloon over 7.00 during his first four months in Triple-A this year.

Former Yankees pitcher Jake Cousins, posing for a photo at Steinbrenner Field during spring training in 2025, was DFA'd by the Dodgers as part of a roster shuffle on Tuesday. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

From Aug. 16 on, however, the left-hander posted a 2.08 ERA over his final seven appearances (39 innings), showing enough promise to reestablish himself as a future piece for the Dodgers — potentially as early as next year.

Serwinoski, another left-hander, has had his own ups and downs (including a 5.31 ERA in Double-A this year) but is still seen as a projectable big-league southpaw further down the line.

Fox was the lone homegrown player of the group, drafted by the Dodgers out of Duke as a 17th-rounder in 2023. He was recovering from Tommy John surgery at the time but has pitched to a 2.91 ERA since his return in 2024, finishing this past season in Double-A.

Cousins, a 32-year-old reliever who hasn't appeared in the big leagues since 2024 with the Yankees because of injuries, was the most notable roster cut, after spending all of last year with the team rehabbing from a 2025 Tommy John procedure.

Cairo, a 25-year-old utilityman who has yet to make his MLB debut, was only acquired in a waiver claim last week from the Mets, who had DFA'd him in their own roster crunch.

#2790. Steelers sign former Seahawks safety

The Pittsburgh Steelers have signed former Seattle Seahawks and Los Angeles Chargers safety AJ Finley to their practice squad, the team announced today.

Finley signed with the Chargers in 2023 as an undrafted free agent out of Ole Miss, where he would go on to appear in 12 games as a rookie. He was waived in November, 2024 and immediately picked up by the Seattle Seahawks. Ever since, he has been on the Seahawks practice squad, appearing in four games in 2025 and three games in 2026. In 29 career games, he has 19 tackles and one forced fumble.

Pittsburgh has been thin at safety all season due to the ongoing injury issues with DeShon Elliott, who missed Week 10 after hurting his knee in warmups. That has left Jaquan Brisker, Robert Spears-Jennings, and Jalen Ramsey in a hybrid role as the only safeties at the Steelers' disposal.

#2789. Chase Home Lending CEO says he plans to 'go on offense' against non-bank lenders

A version of this article first appeared in the CNBC Property Play newsletter with Diana Olick. Property Play covers new and evolving opportunities for the real estate investor, from individuals to venture capitalists, private equity funds, family offices, institutional investors and large public companies. Sign up to receive future editions, straight to your inbox.

Mortgage rates shot higher again last week, after four straight weeks of declines. A hotter-than-expected monthly inflation report, coupled with new headlines from the Iran war, had the average rate on the 30-year fixed mortgage up 33 basis points to 7.5%, according to Mortgage News Daily.

Total mortgage demand, including both refinancing and new purchase loans, is now down just over 39% from a year ago, according to the Mortgage Bankers Association. Despite all that, Sean Grzebin, the CEO of Chase Home Lending, the nation's largest bank mortgage lender, said he's hiring and bent on increasing market share.

"For the last several years, we haven't really added a lot of salespeople," said Grzebin, who admits plainly that this is the worst market in decades. "We're going to hire a bunch of home lending advisors across the United States. We're going to focus on areas where we haven't had a lot of coverage, but there's a lot of mortgage activity and transactions taking place, and it really matches the expansion that we're having within the branch network on the consumer bank side."

Grzebin said he is looking to take back market share from non-bank lenders, who flourished after the financial crisis, when banks were cleaning up from thousands of failed home loans. In August, JPMorganChase announced a 40% increase in home lending capital through 2035 to $750 billion. It will also hire 850 new lending agents.

"I think the non-banks are all very formidable competitors. I think they've had an advantage while the banks were distracted through several decades," he said. "I think now you're starting to see the resurgence of the banks, and I think a lot of banks are actually going to grow their mortgage business through this."

He said he expects to see continued consolidation in the mortgage market, especially as artificial intelligence penetrates the origination part of the business. Chase is investing heavily in AI for underwriting, he said.

"We're hiring mainly in our sales functions because we think we can grow by adding people that have relationships with existing customers today, as well as them taking advantage of our branch network to help grow their business on one side," explained Grzebin. "And we think we can achieve all this growth without adding any incremental headcount in our operation because of the streamlining of the process that's happening with AI."

Chase just ranked highest in mortgage origination satisfaction in J.D. Power's annual U.S. Mortgage Origination Satisfaction Study. While overall satisfaction was unchanged from 2025, just 58% of borrowers said they receive same-day responses from their lender when they ask questions, down from 62% a year ago. Fewer borrowers also say they have a main point of contact, down to 88% from 91% a year ago.

"Borrowers appreciate being able to handle more of the mortgage process online, but they still want someone they can turn to when they have questions," said Bruce Gehrke, senior director of wealth and lending intelligence at J.D. Power in a release. "J.D. Power is seeing less recognition of updates and guidance that are important to keeping borrowers informed. Lenders have an opportunity to make the process more convenient while ensuring borrowers feel supported, especially as they approach closing."

That appears to be what Chase is leaning into as so much of the process now moves to agentic AI. Grzebin said they focused first on the closing process, which customers said was most frustrating. Then they moved to digital tools.

"There's a lot of opportunity there that we're going to be able to take advantage of in the next 12 to 24 months, just making it easier for customers to do more self-service through AI capabilities that we're going to also roll out over the next couple quarters, and so we are invested heavily in AI," he said. "We're going to see some real efficiency from it, and then on the sales side, adding salespeople, but then also figuring out a way to make them more productive."

Servicing is also becoming a bigger factor again, as mortgage delinquencies slowly rise, especially in the FHA space. These low-down-payment loans generally carry higher risk, and as rates stay stubbornly high, those risks are starting to emerge. Chase does do FHA lending, but it is a smaller part of its business.

"I think others are going to have other problems to deal with, particularly if there's any unemployment, and we're just going to be in good shape for that entire cycle. So we look at it as a chance to really go on offense," he said, specifically in FHA lending.

"The non-banks stepped into it and served that market, and that business is being challenged right now, and so we're trying to get back into it," Grzebin said. "We'd love to get back into it. We're priced competitively to get back into it."

#2788. Top Wall Street analysts are upbeat about the prospects for these 3 stocks

September ended on a mixed note, with the S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average falling 0.5% and 4.3%, respectively, while the Nasdaq Composite climbed 1.9%. Markets remained on edge with higher oil prices and Treasury yields weighing on investor sentiment and triggering fears of additional interest rate hikes.

In this climate, investors with a long-term horizon can track recommendations from top Wall Street analysts to find stocks with solid growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Neocloud company CoreWeave (CRWV) is benefiting from robust AI-driven demand for cloud computing.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Samik Chatterjee upgraded CoreWeave to buy from hold, raising his price target to $125 from $120. The analyst cited favorable pricing, driven by solid demand, for the upgrade.

Chatterjee said CoreWeave is now more willing to sign premium priced, shorter-term contracts, which lift his revenue and margin estimates. CRWV stock performance has been underwhelming as investors worry about high capital spending, the analyst noted. That said, he believes that higher prices and increased margins will more than offset the rise in debt to fund capital spending, improving the stock's valuation.

Chatterjee noted the 25% price hike in July across CoreWeave's product offerings and frequent price increases by rivals like Nebius (NBIS), adding that some peers are charging nearly three times more for short-term compute contracts than what CoreWeave charges on longer-term deals.

"CoreWeave's leverage of the opportunity [is] evident in its recent press release highlighting contracts signed in F3Q at ~$40M/MW," said Chatterjee. JPMorgan said higher pricing is already contributing 5-10 percentage points to contract margins versus earlier deals.

Chatterjee ranks No. 4 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 75% of the time, delivering an average return of 55.3%. See CoreWeave Statistics on TipRanks.

Palo Alto Networks (PANW) is a provider of cybersecurity solutions across network, cloud, security operations, AI and identity. Following a discussion with management, BTIG analyst Gray Powell reiterated a buy rating on PANW and raised his price target to $425 from $404.

After a review of Palo Alto's product-level growth catalysts, Powell sees clear upside to both revenue and next-generation security (NGS) annual recurring revenue (ARR) in fiscal 2027. He expects PANW to deliver pro forma revenue growth of more than 17%, above the consensus estimate of 14.6%, and NGS ARR growth of more than 26% versus the Street's expectation of 24.5%.

Over the past five years, the Palo Alto business mix, has moved to higher-growth product areas and new markets. from what was earlier viewed as a hardware-centric network security company, Powell said. The 5-star analyst estimates that hardware firewalls and attached subscriptions now account for less than 50% of revenue.

Meanwhile, high-growth solutions, including Prisma SASE, software firewalls, Cortex XSIAM, Chronosphere and Idira now represent more than 35% of PANW's revenue. "Investors may not fully realize the growth potential within PANW's platform story," Powell said.

BTIG is optimistic about the prospects of Chronosphere in observability, CyberArk in identity and software firewalls and Prisma AIRS in the network and AI security business.

Powell ranks No. 320 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 17.3%. See Palo Alto Networks KPIs on TipRanks.

In a research note on September 30, Rosenblatt analyst Scott Devitt reiterated a buy rating on e-commerce and cloud computing platform Amazon (AMZN), raising his price target to $360 from $335. Fears that agentic commerce poses a major threat to Amazon's advertising business seem "overstated," he said.

Devitt noted that the growing adoption of personal agents and AI shopping assistants has sparked concern about displacement of Amazon's retail media business. But the 5-star analyst remains bullish, given Amazon's ability to adapt to changing consumer behavior, as it's done in the past.

Additionally, Devitt argues that even if an AI agent helps a shopper discover a product, Amazon can still handle the purchase and delivery, keeping much of the transaction value. He highlighted that management believes that existing Amazon shoppers will likely continue to start their shopping journey on the platform because it has better personalization, accurate pricing and inventory information that third-party agents still lack.

Overall, Devitt expects Amazon's retail business to prove a net beneficiary of agentic commerce. Fewer clicks would not hurt the company if the value of each remaining click and overall volumes keep rising, he said. The argument is that even if Amazon loses some sponsored ad revenue over the next few years, it would likely be only a small part of the company's total business and could be offset by growth in other areas.

"Consistent with our AI framework, we continue to view Amazon as one of the most resilient leaders in our coverage, combining owned compute infrastructure with a physical fulfillment moat," said Devitt. His bullish view is supported, Devitt said, by expectations that Amazon Web Services (AWS) will exit the year at a more than 45% growth rate with continued margin improvement.

Devitt ranks No. 765 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 49% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Amazon Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2786. Dodgers protect four prospects from Rule 5 draft, including top pitcher snatched from Yankees

There might not be a Rule 5 draft in Major League Baseball this winter, with an upcoming lockout threatening to cancel the annual event.

But the Dodgers didn't take any chances on Tuesday, still electing to protect four minor-league prospects who would've been Rule 5 eligible.

In a series of roster moves Tuesday, the Dodgers added pitchers Christian Zazueta, Jackson Ferris, Adam Serwinoski and Luke Fox to their 40-man roster, while designating for assignment pitcher Jake Cousins and recent waiver claim infielder Christian Cairo.

The Dodgers' reigning Minor League Pitcher of the Year Christian Zazueta is continuing his rise through the franchise's minor-league system. MLB Photos via Getty Images

The moves ensure none of the four young pitchers will be subject to a possible Rule 5 draft — in which non-40-man players who have accrued a certain amount of time in their organizations become available for other teams to "draft" and add to their own roster — while also subjecting Cousins (a veteran reliever recovering from Tommy John surgery) and Cairo (who was claimed from the Mets last week) to waivers if they are to remain in the organization.

The last time MLB experienced an offseason lockout, during the 2021-22 offseason, the Rule 5 draft (which is usually held during the winter meetings in December) was canceled and not rescheduled.

It's possible that could be the case again this year, with the league's current CBA set to expire on Dec. 1. But last week, general manager Brandon Gomes said the club would operate as if things were "business as usual," leading the Dodgers to protect their four prospects ahead of a Tuesday afternoon deadline.

Brandon Gomes, general manager of the Los Angeles Dodgers, speaks with media during the MLB general manager meetings in Las Vegas, Tuesday, Nov. 10, 2026. AP Photo/John Locher

The most obvious choice was Zazueta, a 22-year-old right-handed starter whom the Dodgers acquired for reliever Caleb Ferguson from the Yankees three years ago in what has become a steal of a transaction.

A little-known talent at that time, Zazueta has since blossomed into one of the most promising young arms in baseball. He is ranked as the top pitcher (and fifth overall player) in the Dodgers' farm system. He is the No. 33 overall prospect in the sport, according to MLB Pipeline. And he is coming off a strong 2026 campaign in which he posted a 3.44 ERA in his first stint in Double-A and won the club's Minor League Pitcher of the Year award for a second straight season.

Serwinoski and Ferris, two other trade acquisitions in recent years, were also added to the 40-man roster as expected.

Ferris has been on a bit of a roller coaster since joining the organization in the Michael Busch trade from the Cubs, along with since-traded outfielder Zyhir Hope (who was a key part of the deadline blockbuster for Tarik Skubal this past summer). The 22-year-old had impressive stints in Double-A in 2024 and 2025, but then saw his ERA balloon over 7.00 during his first four months in Triple-A this year.

Former Yankees pitcher Jake Cousins, posing for a photo at Steinbrenner Field during spring training in 2025, was DFA'd by the Dodgers as part of a roster shuffle on Tuesday. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

From Aug. 16 on, however, the left-hander posted a 2.08 ERA over his final seven appearances (39 innings), showing enough promise to reestablish himself as a future piece for the Dodgers — potentially as early as next year.

Serwinoski, another left-hander, has had his own ups and downs (including a 5.31 ERA in Double-A this year) but is still seen as a projectable big-league southpaw further down the line.

Fox was the lone homegrown player of the group, drafted by the Dodgers out of Duke as a 17th-rounder in 2023. He was recovering from Tommy John surgery at the time but has pitched to a 2.91 ERA since his return in 2024, finishing this past season in Double-A.

Cousins, a 32-year-old reliever who hasn't appeared in the big leagues since 2024 with the Yankees because of injuries, was the most notable roster cut, after spending all of last year with the team rehabbing from a 2025 Tommy John procedure.

Cairo, a 25-year-old utilityman who has yet to make his MLB debut, was only acquired in a waiver claim last week from the Mets, who had DFA'd him in their own roster crunch.

#2785. Packers Film Room: The Tucky Sneak adds a wrinkle

Since his second year in the league, tight end Tucker Kraft has been something of a battering ram in the sneak game. Since 2024, the Green Bay Packers have used Kraft as the ball carrier in 8 sneak attempts: 3 times on 4th & 1, 5 times on 3rd & 1. He has converted 7 of those attempts, for a Success Rate of 87.5%. The only time he has been stopped was on the 3rd & 1 play against the New England Patriots in Week 9 of this season. His longest gain came in the 2024 Wild Card game, picking up 8 yards on 3rd & 1.

So it would make sense for the Packers to have a variation or two in their bag. We've seen a couple of nice ones over the past couple of years from other teams; who could forget Mark Andrews ' 35-yard TD scamper on 4th & 1 off a spin?

So far, the Packers have kept it pretty vanilla. And why not? After all, an 87.5% Success Rate is exactly what you're looking for in that situation. Take your battering ram and shove him into the line until he gets at least a yard. There's not always a need to overcomplicate things.

However, you need the occasional curveball. You don't want the defense being able to load up in the middle of the line, but there are also explosives to be had if you can catch them out of position. That's what the Packers did in the 1st quarter this past weekend.

Down 3-0 with 8:52 remaining in the 1st quarter, the Packers were facing 3rd & 1 at the Vikings' 44-yard line. They come out in 11 personnel (1 RB, 1 TE, 3 WR), starting in an empty, condensed 3×2 look with Tucker Kraft [85] wide on the left. The Vikings have 4 men up on the line, with 3 linebackers set a few yards behind them.

Kraft shifts under center, and it creates motion on the Vikings side of the ball. Eric Wilson [55] comes up to the line and moves the DT to his side over and Andrew Van Ginkel [43] leaves his spot over Matthew Golden [0] to get up on the line. Ivan Pace Jr. [0] starts moving towards the interior of the line as well.

Everything is condensed to the middle, and the Packers are looking to play off that. At the snap, Kraft pitches back to Jordan Love [10], who rolls right. Chris Brooks [30] is shading out on the right side of the line. He's acting as a personal protector for Love on that side.

Golden slow-plays his release, then breaks on a corner route while Christian Watson [9] works as the trailer from the left.

This is supposed to go to Golden. If Love throws this directly after he gathers the ball, he likely has Golden for a nice gain. However, Isaiah Rodgers [2] is playing as the boundary on that side. He's initially held close to the line (in case of a Love scramble), but after Love is steered back inside by Van Ginkel, Rodgers breaks to the corner, taking away Golden. Love may think the throw to Golden would need to be behind him, and thus harder to corral.

So Love sets and fires to Watson in the middle of the field. Watson does not appear to ever truly be expecting this ball. He looks like he's slow-playing his release in the same way Golden does, but he never looks to hit a different gear or to break sharply and create a little more separation.

Love finds him all the same, and the Packers pick up 15 yards and the 1st down.

And if we're opening up the floor for other ideas off of this, I humbly offer up the Fake Sneak/TE Pop.

Albums listened to: Sister Ray Davies – Holy Island; Phoebe Bridgers – Lost Weekend; Hannah Cole – Switchbacks; U2 – Carnaval De Luz

#2784. Junk bonds are 'flashing yellow.' Watch these warning signs

Cracks are forming in the junk bond market as investors demand higher payouts for owning the market's riskiest debt. It isn't time to ditch high-yield bonds, but investors should pay attention to the warning signs.

High-yield bonds now yield 8.1%, up from 7.22% a month ago. The increase reflects a jump in yields across the curve as investors bake in more inflation from high energy prices and other pressures, including concern about the deficit — hitting nearly $2 trillion in the fiscal year that ended Sept. 30.

The high-yield market is also showing stress on the credit side with spreads recently widening to levels not seen since April, according to the Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis. Credit spreads are the difference in yield between the bonds and Treasurys of similar maturities. Wider spreads mean investors are demanding higher yields for holding corporate debt, viewing it as riskier.

Spreads are at 315 basis points in the overall high-yield market, higher than a year ago but still below levels in March when they reached 346 bps. One basis point equals one one-hundredth of a percent, or 0.01%.

The high-yield market consists of bonds rated BB+ by S&P and Fitch and Ba1 and under by Moody's. The lowest-rated cohort, CCC and below, has seen the most movement with spreads climbing dramatically over the past year to roughly 1,250 bps.

Right now, the high-yield market is "flashing yellow" but is "far from red," said Michael Arone, chief investment strategist at State Street Investment Management.

It makes sense that investors are demanding more compensation for taking on additional credit risk as borrowing costs rise, he said.

Yields are elevated across the board with the 10-year Treasury reaching its highest level since 2002 earlier in the week.

"The bigger question is whether this is simply a repricing of interest rate risk, or the beginning of a more fundamental reassessment of credit quality," Arone said.

He's in a wait-and-see mode since earnings are still growing, interest-coverage ratios remain good, and while default rates have ticked up some, he believes it is not concerning.

That said, the starting point in spreads is likely weighing on investors' psyche, since they are still low by historical standards.

"There's a small margin of error here, which I also think raises the anxiety level," Arone explained. "The compensation that investors are receiving for taking on this credit risk isn't overwhelming relative to history, and therefore subtle changes in credit spreads can be concerning."

While there may be concerns about part of the lower-rated market, the overall high-yield market is in good fundamental shape.

In fact, credit quality is at a record high, with BB bonds making up over 60% of the market compared with 38% prior to the global financial crisis, said Kelley Gerrity, a fixed income strategist at Morgan Stanley Investment Management.

"We've had higher-quality companies coming in, and with higher rates now, you also have more discipline from companies that are more indebted ... just because of the higher cost of capital, so that actually is creating a bit of a healthier picture as well," she said.

The lowest tier of high yield has always been more speculative, since the bonds have a higher default risk. Therefore, it's no surprise that they are the ones seeing the most widening in spreads, said Collin Martin, head of fixed income research and strategy at the Schwab Center for Financial Research.

The overall move in high-yield spreads have been "orderly," he said. "Are there cracks forming? I think the cracks are logical cracks with the lowest-rated bonds and it's too early to say that it's spreading to the broad credit market."

In addition, fluctuations within the CCC-rated and lower spreads have been idiosyncratic, Gerrity added. Morgan Stanley recently broke down the spreads in the lowest cohort into two buckets: performing assets and non-performing assets, which are defined as spreads above 1,000 bps.

The non-performing spread to worst is 2,818 bps, while the performing bucket — the biggest piece of the CCC market — is 461 bps, she said.

"It is isolated," she said. "I'm not necessarily sure that that will go away anytime soon, but it doesn't tell the story of overall concern and flashing caution for credit markets right now."

For now, investors should remain selective within higher yield, Gerrity advised.

"We're looking for the best relative value opportunities, and we think that exists within that single-B cohort, and so we're leaning into that," she said.

Investors should be concerned if there is a steep widening in the broader high-yield market.

But there's scant evidence of stress in the BB cohort. Spreads in that group are at 194 bps, up from 179 bps a year ago — although the move hasn't been in a straight line.

"We're going to be focusing our attention on what are considered some stronger businesses," Martin said. "If we start to see the markets demand higher spreads there also, that's what we'll be looking at to see if risks are really rising."

It's also important to understand that the Federal Reserve is raising rates in a strong economic environment, said R.J. Gallo, chief investment officer of global fixed income at Federated Hermes. The central bank is concerned about an inflation problem amid high fossil fuel prices due to the Iran war, while growth has been stronger than expected, he noted.

"With growth being good as the reason that the Fed is hiking, then you wouldn't expect high yield to blow out because growth means revenues stay up, means cash flow and profitability stays up," he explained.

High yield becomes a disaster when the economy is heading into a sharp economic downturn, Gallo said.

"That's when spreads really widen out," he said. "But a recession is not the odds-on bet. Now, talk to me in six months if the Fed keeps hiking over and over, if oil prices stay high for longer, well, then maybe we will start to wonder."

#2782. Warriors Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg’s Trash Talk Gets Honest Response From Former Coach Dusty May

SAN FRANCISCO, CALIFORNIA - NOVEMBER 09: Yaxel Lendeborg #1 of the Golden State Warriors talks to Derik Queen #22 of the New Orleans Pelicans before they both received a technical foul in the fourth quarter at Chase Center on November 09, 2026 in San Francisco, California. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Lachlan Cunningham/Getty Images) Getty

Golden State Warriors fans found out who Yaxel Lendeborg was in his very first NBA game. The rookie forward went back and forth with Lakers star Luka Doncic in a 127-105 loss, picked up a technical foul, and later told reporters he had lost respect for him.

That night has followed the No. 11 pick ever since. Now, with the Warriors set to visit the Dallas Mavericks on Wednesday, the man who coached him to a national title has been asked about it.

Mavericks head coach Dusty May had Lendeborg at Michigan last season. Mike Curtis of The Dallas Morning News shared a video on X in which May was asked whether he saw the same trash talk in college.

"Oh yeah, yeah. We saw it a lot," May said.

May also saw the draft from the inside. Dallas passed on Lendeborg at No. 9 in June, and Golden State took him two picks later.

"During the draft process, some people really appreciated it, but some were probably a little fearful of how transparent Yaxel is," May said. "There's no fluff, there's no coach speak. He's been media trained, but he just decides to be authentically who he is."

"You're never going to wonder where you stand with Yax. You're never going to wonder what he's thinking because he's going to let you know," May added. "That's what makes him someone that everyone enjoys being around."

Warriors head coach Steve Kerr has taken the same view. After the Doncic exchange, he said he loved that his rookie did not back down, but told him to back up his words.

GettyYaxel Lendeborg #1 of the Golden State Warriors talks to Derik Queen #22 of the New Orleans Pelicans

For most of the season, Lendeborg has done that, and May has noticed. Asked if knowing his game makes him easier to plan for, he said no.

"We had to beg him to shoot threes last year and finally he did," May said. "Now he's shooting more threes, he's creating off the bounce, he's shooting more threes off the dribble."

"He's very proud of his progression. And they've done a nice job with how they're using him as well," May added.

The numbers show that. Lendeborg is averaging more than 20 points and seven rebounds a game, and he was No. 1 on NBA.com's rookie ladder last week.

He also dropped 37 points on the LA Clippers on Nov. 1, the most by a Warriors rookie since Stephen Curry in 2010.

The last week has been harder. Lendeborg scored three points on 1-of-13 shooting in a 123-85 loss to the San Antonio Spurs, then put up 28 in Monday's loss to the New Orleans Pelicans. The Warriors are 8-6 and have lost three straight after an eight-game win streak.

Wednesday in Dallas is the next chance to stop that slide. It is also Lendeborg's first regular-season game against his old coach.

#2780. Lisa Cook lawyer signals Fed governor won't attend Trump committee's hearing

Federal Reserve Governor Lisa Cook's attorney on Tuesday gave the strongest signal yet that his client will not attend a hearing at the White House with a "committee of inquiry" investigating mortgage fraud allegations against Cook.

The committee established last month by President Donald Trump, who has previously tried to remove Cook from the central bank's board, is pretextual and poses a threat to the Fed, attorney Abbe Lowell wrote in a letter to presidential counsel Will Scharf.

"Governor Cook has no legal obligation to participate in an unfair hearing — and indeed, has a duty to the Federal Reserve to decline to participate in any proceeding that would undermine the institution's unique status and independence," Lowell wrote in the letter, which is dated Monday and was made public Tuesday.

The latest correspondence came nearly two weeks after the White House committee delayed its proposed hearing with Cook to Dec. 14 from Nov. 5. Lowell said that any hearing with Cook could not take place until at least December because of conflicts with her Fed duties.

While Lowell had raised strong concerns from the outset about the legitimacy of the committee, he initially said Cook "welcomes the opportunity to present the facts so she can clear her name."

But he has continued to cast doubts on both the committee's purpose and its composition of three executive branch officials, including National Economic Council Director Kevin Hassett.

Lowell's tone sounded more hostile than ever in this week's letter, the first known to be sent by either party in the matter since the Republican Party's devastating losses in the midterm elections on Nov. 3.

He accused Scharf and the committee of seeking "only to manufacture 'cause' for a predetermined removal outcome."

Lowell highlighted two top concerns. First, he asserted that Hassett "is not competent to serve on any factfinding committee" because his prior comments about Cook "demonstrate that he has prejudged the issue."

Second, Lowell called it "fundamentally unfair" that the committee is demanding Cook hand over documents "immediately," while pledging to provide the governor with its own records just seven days before the hearing.

"Your and the Committee's failure to address these legitimate issues, over multiple weeks of back-and-forth correspondence, reinforces my prior observation: President Trump's decision to fire Governor Cook has already been made," Lowell wrote.

"Consequently, this Committee process appears to be a façade designed to obscure a predetermined decision to fire Governor Cook or to unreasonably manufacture a supposed 'adverse inference' if she does not participate in a process that does not comport with the requirements of a fair hearing," the lawyer wrote.

Lowell told Scharf that Cook would still be prepared to appear for a hearing if the White House "implements fair procedures." But the "ultimatums" Scharf gave in his last reply in early November "do not provide a productive path forward to enabling that participation," Lowell wrote.

The White House did not immediately respond to CNBC's request for comment on Lowell's letter.

Trump, who frequently urges the Fed to lower interest rates, has long sought to fire Cook, an appointee of former President Joe Biden.

Cook, the first Black woman to serve on the Fed's Board of Governors, last voted to cut rates in December 2025, siding with the majority. The Fed unanimously voted to raise rates after its meeting in September.

Trump officials sent a criminal referral last year alleging Cook may have committed mortgage fraud by falsely claiming different properties as her primary residence in order to get more favorable terms. Cook adamantly denied wrongdoing, and, after Trump tried to fire her in August 2025, filed a lawsuit to block her removal.

The Supreme Court in June prevented Trump from firing Cook, but gave him a path to try again if additional legal steps were taken. In August, the White House told Cook it was reviving its efforts to fire her on mortgage-related grounds. In October, Trump signed a presidential memo establishing the committee of inquiry to investigate allegations that Cook lied on records related to "one or more mortgage instruments."

#2779. Sixers single out VJ Edgecombe for impact in other areas after the win

NEW YORK -- It's been documented a number of times throughout the 2026-27 season, but VJ Edgecombe has continued to make an impact for the Philadelphia 76ers despite not being involved as much in terms of his usage percentage as a rookie in the previous year.

Edgecombe had 14 points, nine rebounds, four assists, and two steals in Monday's win over the Brooklyn Nets and he was impactful on the defensive end of the floor. He set the tone in the rebounding department and did a number of great things to contribute to winning for Philadelphia.

"I was most impressed with the way he was getting through and over screens," coach Nick Nurse said of Edgecombe. "He just was constantly getting screened and he was just bodying through them, keeping his man right there, just sticking to him really closely. And I thought that was a good tone setting part of our defense by him."

Getting through and over screens is something that will not be shown in the box score. It's not a contribution that can be measured by any type of numbers, but Edgecombe was able to get over screens and bother the Nets out on the perimeter. His point of attack defense has been something that has been noticeable on a nightly basis while also giving Philadelphia a big boost in the rebounding department.

Edgecombe had five rebounds in the first quarter as the Sixers were able to set the tone against a young, but scrappy Nets team. He has been a real help in that area and Philadelphia has benefited from his improvement in that area.

"That's an emphasis area for me," Edgecombe explained. "I just try to get as much rebound as I can kind of try to speak selfishly. I'm able to obviously start the break or push the ball, do whatever I got to do, or just go coast to coast. So, selfishly, I like getting rebounds."

The Sixers will continue to push Edgecombe forward as they grow as a team and continue to build chemistry and nail down roles. Their next chance will be on Wednesday against the Orlando Magic to finish a road trip.

#2778. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Uncertainty in the Middle East, elevated Treasury yields and fears about an AI bubble continue to keep global markets on edge. Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking stable income can consider enhancing their portfolios by adding dividend-paying stocks.

However, the vast universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Tracking the ratings of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as their recommendations are backed by in-depth research.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Chord Energy is an independent exploration and production company with long-lived assets primarily in the Williston Basin. With a quarterly base dividend of $1.30 per share, CHRD offers a dividend yield of 3.67%.

In a research note on third-quarter expectations, RBC Capital analyst Scott Hanold reiterated a buy rating on CHRD stock with a price target of $175. "We model production near the top of guidance and continue to see strong performance from new wells and base production," said Hanold.

Specifically, the five-star analyst expects Q3 2026 production at 283 thousand barrels of oil equivalent per day, or Mboe/d, in line with the Street's consensus and near the high end of the 278 to 284 Mboe/d guidance. Hanold said his Q3 2026 earnings per share estimate increased to $5.29 due to lower non-cash depreciation, depletion and amortization.

Meanwhile, cash flow per share estimate moved lower to $13.59 to reflect Hanold's update based on the current backdrop and commodity mark-to-market. He expects Chord Energy to report capital spending of $375 million for Q3 2026, slightly above the Street's estimate of $369 million.

Overall, Hanold remains bullish on CHRD, given its best-in-class balance sheet. He expects a free cash flow payout of 80% in the third quarter, with the possibility of an upside. Hanold expects investors to focus on management commentary on 2027 capital plans, Williston M&A opportunities, and long-term performance of 4-mile wells.

Hanold ranks No. 310 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 14.9%. See Chord Energy KPIs on TipRanks.

Williams, an energy infrastructure company that delivers natural gas, is the next dividend pick. The company recently completed the $5.5 billion acquisition of Momentum Midstream, which will expand its natural gas infrastructure in the Haynesville to meet growing Gulf Coast LNG, power and industrial demand. At a quarterly dividend of $0.5250 per share, Williams offers a yield of 2.89%.

In her latest research note, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $87. Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, the analyst said that she expects WMB to announce strong quarterly performance.

Scotto raised her estimates to reflect insights from the quarterly catch-up call, the completion of the Momentum Midstream acquisition, and mark-to-market quarter-to-date commodity prices. She expects WMB to report Q3 2026 adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization of $2,036 million, which is modestly above the Street's consensus estimate.

Furthermore, Scotto expects Williams' Transmission, Power & Gulf segment to increase quarter-over-quarter, driven by the startup of the Socrates power project and favorable natural gas liquids, or NGL, margins.

Scotto noted that WMB stock has underperformed due to backlash related to data centers and investor anticipation of another Power Innovation project. She expects the company to announce at least one more project before the end of this year, potentially with a new customer and a contract duration of 15 to 20 years, which would be a positive catalyst for the stock.

Scotto ranks No. 175 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been profitable 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.2%. See Williams Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

EOG Resources, a crude oil and natural gas exploration and production company, will hold its third-quarter earnings call on Nov. 6. At a quarterly dividend of $1.02 per share, EOG offers a dividend yield of 2.75%.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Jefferies analyst Lloyd Byrne reiterated a buy rating on EOG Resources stock and increased his price target to $185 from $175. The analyst expects the company's Q3 cash flow per share to exceed the Street's consensus by 13%, driven by better-than-expected production.

Byrne expects crude oil production, excluding the UAE, to reach 551 Mboe/d, which is at the high end of guidance (546-551 Mboe/d) and above the Street's projection of 548 Mboe/d, driven by continued growth in the Utica.

Furthermore, Byrne expects cash operating expenses to come in at $10.27 per barrel of oil equivalent, below the midpoint of guidance of $10.05 to $11.35, helped by lower lease operating expenses. Overall, the analyst expects EOG to deliver free cash flow of $2.65 billion, supporting another quarter of buybacks near the minimum 70% shareholder return threshold and implying about $1.5 billion in share repurchases.

"As a first indication of the '27 plan, we expect EOG will message another year of mid-single-digit (~3.5%) growth on a flat capital budget (~$6.55bn), which is above the Street modeling 1% oil growth at $6.6bn," said Byrne.

Byrne ranks No. 303 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 18.5%. See EOG Resources Statistics on TipRanks.
