#2777. NFL Injury Report, Week 11: Latest news, updates for Drake Maye, Kenneth Walker III, Brock Bowers, more players for fantasy football

It was a rough week for the New England Patriots over in Germany. As we look over the Week 11 NFL injury report, the Patriots are going to pop up quite a bit going into their bye. We also have five other teams on bye — the Packers, Seahawks, Browns, Falcons and Rams. Those teams will hopefully get healthier over that span.

Below, we'll go over the latest injury news, providing updates on key players for fantasy football.

Drake Maye, NE (ankle) — Out indefinitely

Baker Mayfield, TB (lower leg) — Could miss Week 11

Maye: New England heads into its bye, though the season may be lost coming out of it. At this point, it's feared Maye has a fractured ankle and could need season-ending surgery. At the very least, he could miss multiple games or be placed on IR. It's almost helpful that New England is on bye in Week 11, as fantasy managers would already be looking for a Maye replacement or streaming option.

If Maye is done for the season, it's difficult to trust anyone on this offense. There are injuries and ailments everywhere, from A.J. Brown to Rhamondre Stevenson and TreVeyon Henderson. The Pats are well behind the 8-1 Bills in the AFC East, though are still in the wild-card picture. But if Maye is out, that outlook could change fast. If you have any skill position player on New England, be sure to have contingency plans. This team could turn into a fantasy wasteland soon.

Mayfield: The Bucs are coming out of the Week 10 bye but may be without Mayfield. He suffered a lower leg injury in Week 9's loss to Chicago, and the extra rest didn't appear to help. If Mayfield is out against Detroit in Week 11, Jalon Daniels, who started three games earlier in the season, would get another shot. With so many teams on bye and Maye managers searching for answers, Daniels isn't a bad waiver add. Tampa Bay has a pretty soft schedule the rest of the season. Expect the Bucs to continue to ride the running game with Bucky Irving and Kenny Gainwell.

Jacory Croskey-Merritt, WAS (groin) — Could return from IR

Walker: The Chiefs added Walker to the injury report last Friday and he was swiftly ruled out for Week 10 against the Falcons. As a result, rookie Emmett Johnson thrived in lead-back duties, totaling 171 yards on 28 touches (six catches) with two TDs for 32.1 fantasy points. Reports suggest this will be a short absence for Walker and he should be back Week 11 against the Cardinals.

If Walker is back, he should return to his role as the lead back and get the majority of the work. Johnson has played well enough to warrant touches, however, and could get extra work given the opponent. Kansas City is a heavy favorite in Week 11, and if the team pulls away, we could see Walker rest and Johnson get work in garbage time. If Walker is active, you're playing him, of course. But if you were able to snag Johnson off the wire, he's also in play as a flex option.

Texans RBs: Marks was forced to leave Week 10 against the Browns due to a concussion. He's unlikely to go Thursday against the Colts. Head coach DeMeco Ryans also doesn't know if Montgomery will be ready on the short week. If both backs are out, it's looking like Emari Demercado and Dare Ogunbowale will split the work out of the backfield.

While it's a good matchup, if both Marks and Monty are out, neither depth back is a strong fantasy play. Each would be a risky flex option. Instead, expect the Texans to lean on the passing game, which has looked good over the past four games. Xavier Hutchinson, Tank Dell and Kayshon Boutte remain interesting adds off the waiver wire depending on league size/format. If Montgomery can play, he's in the RB2/3 range given all the teams on bye. It's also a great matchup, but he could be eased back into action.

Luther Burden III, CHI (concussion) — Cleared protocol

Wan'Dale Robinson, TEN (ankle) — placed on IR

Burden: The Bears were on bye in Week 10 and Burden has had ample time to get right. Head coach Ben Johnson told reporters on Tuesday that Burden had cleared concussion protocol and is good to go for Week 11 against the Saints. With Burden out there, it's all system's go for the Bears coming off the bye.

Evans: The Niners may not be in a rush to get Evans back given the 46 points the offense scored on Sunday against the Cowboys. The veteran wideout missed his third straight game but will have a shot at returning in Week 11 against the Vikings, not an ideal spot. San Francisco also has a pretty crowded receiving room with Deebo Samuel Sr. returning and Keenan Allen coming over via trade, not to mention George Kittle and the emerging Jordan Watkins. If Evans can get back in Week 11, he's a low-upside WR2/3 given the matchup.

W. Robinson: The Titans placed the wideout on IR and he's out for at least the next four games. He had just three catches on four targets for 10 yards in Week 10 against the Jaguars. Elic Ayomanor should get a nice boost with Robinson out, though Tate could return against the Cowboys on Sunday. Ayomanor had 5-79-0 on 10 targets against Jacksonville. He's a decent waiver wire add this week considering the matchup and lack of depth at WR for Tennessee.

Bowers: Reports came out that Bowers avoided a major injury in Week 10's loss to the Seahawks, which is good news. He's considered day-to-day and has a shot to play in Week 11 against the Broncos. The tight end didn't get much time to develop chemistry with new starting QB Fernando Mendoza on Sunday, catching just two passes for 18 yards before exiting. With Mendoza under center, it's a slight downgrade for Bowers, but he's still a locked-in TE1. If Bowers ends up missing the game, Michael Mayer isn't a bad streaming option. The Broncos have been a soft matchup for TEs this season.

#2776. Editor-in-chief mailbag: Sixers are 12-3 and haven’t played their best ball yet

With their win over the Brooklyn Nets Monday, the Sixers swept another back-to-back and moved to 12-3, their best start since the 2000-01 season.

If you were told before the season started they'd be 12-3 after 15 games, you would've done cartwheels. There are obviously things to figure out. The half-court offense has been ugly at times, though it's trending upwards, ranking 14th in offensive rating. The defense has gone the other way, but remains a top-10 unit.

The most notable thing about the Sixers through 15 games is how they're valuing possessions. Rebounding has been a constant pain point under Nick Nurse, but they're currently fourth in defensive rebounding percentage. They're also second in turnover percentage and lead the league in loose balls recovered per game. They're also undefeated in clutch games … though they sort of created their own clutch game Monday.

How are you feeling so far? What are your biggest questions? Hit me up in the comments and I'll do my best to respond to as many of you as possible.

#2774. Astros DH Yordan Alvarez Wins Second Silver Slugger Award

HOUSTON, TX – Louisville Slugger announced tonight that Astros designated hitter Yordan Alvarez has won his second career American League Silver Slugger Award. Additionally, it was also announced that the Astros have won the AL Silver Slugger Team Award for the first time in franchise history.

Established in 1980, the Louisville Slugger Silver Slugger Awards honor the best offensive producers at each position in both the American and National Leagues. Positional winners are determined by a vote of managers and coaches who cast ballots in their respective leagues for players at each position. The Silver Slugger Team Award was added in 2023.

Alvarez, who was named as the AL MVP last week, batted .316 (179×567) with 105 runs, 34 doubles, one triple, 42 home runs, 106 RBI, 109 walks and a 1.033 OPS in 158 games. Alvarez won the American League and Major League batting titles, becoming the third player in franchise history to win a batting title, joining 1B Yuli Gurriel (2021) and 2B Jose Altuve (2014, 2016-17). He also led the AL in OPS, slugging percentage (.601), on-base percentage (.432), total bases (341), walks (109), intentional walks (33), wRC+ (181) OPS+ (190) and extra-base hits (77), while also ranking tied for second in home runs, second in RBI, second in hits, fourth in doubles and fourth in runs scored. Additionally, Alvarez was the only player in the Majors this season to post an OPS over 1.000.

He became just the third player since 1970 to win the AL batting title while hitting 40-or-more home runs, joining NYY OF Aaron Judge (2025) and DET 1B Miguel Cabrera (2012-13). This season, Alvarez won three AL Player of the Week honors along with two AL Player of the Month honors. Alvarez was also selected to his fourth career All-Star Game, and it was the second time that he was voted as a starter (also, 2024). In addition to earning AL MVP honors, Alvarez also won the AL Hank Aaron Award, the AL Comeback Player of the Year, the Edgar Martinez Outstanding Designated Hitter Award and was an All-MLB First Team selection. He also was named as the Astros MVP by the Houston Chapter of the BBWAA.

In their franchise history, the Astros have 14 Silver Slugger Award winners who have now combined for a total of 29 awards overall: Altuve (2014-18, 2022, 2024), OF Kyle Tucker (2023), DH Yordan Alvarez (2022, 2026), 3B Alex Bregman (2019), OF George Springer (2017, 2019), OF Carlos Lee (2007), 3B Morgan Ensberg (2005), LHP Mike Hampton (1999), OF Moises Alou (1998), 1B Jeff Bagwell (1994, 1997, 1999), Craig Biggio (catcher in 1989, second baseman in 1994-95, 1997-98), 1B Glenn Davis (1986), OF José Cruz (1983-84) and SS Dickie Thon (1983).

As a team in 2026, the Astros led the Majors in home runs with 226 and ranked first in the AL in OPS (.733) and slugging percentage (.417). This year was the first time that the Astros led the Majors in home runs since 2000, hitting 249 home runs that season.

#2773. Warriors are ‘putting too much pressure’ on rookie Yaxel Lendeborg, ‘It’s unfair’

NEW ORLEANS — Yaxel Lendeborg, the typically talkative Warriors rookie, was given the night off Monday from his usual postgame media obligations.

He didn't need to say a word to convey his feelings in Golden State's 115-94 loss to the Pelicans. His body language came with as much candor as anything to escape his mouth.

Lendeborg poured in a team-high 28 points, but almost all of them came after the Warriors had dug themselves into a 31-point hole to a team that had lost its previous six games.

Golden State Warriors forward Yaxel Lendeborg (1) reacts during the fourth quarter of an NBA game against the New Orleans Pelicans on Monday, Nov. 16, 2026. Getty Images

He spent the final minutes of the loss sitting on the bench, head hanging, with a towel covering his newly buzzed head of hair. Draymond Green picked up the slack with reporters after the game, and said that's exactly what the Warriors need to do to help the rookie on the court.

"[We need to] depend on him, but lower the expectations," Green said.

Fourteen games into his NBA career, the 24-year-old Michigan product is being asked to be the primary scorer in an offense with precious few options without Stephen Curry.

"It's unfair to him to try to make him step into Steph's role 13 games into his career. So depend on him like we need to, but lower what we expect of him to allow things to just flow into him.

"I think, right now, we're putting too much pressure on him."

Golden State Warriors' Draymond Green, left, is fouled by New Orleans Pelicans' Jordan Poole on Monday, Nov. 16, 2026, at Smoothie King Center in New Orleans. Getty Images

All eyes were already on Lendeborg as soon as he stepped into Smoothie King Center. Taking the court for warm-ups, he unraveled a durag on his head and revealed his brand-new, chopped-off hair.

Lendeborg's decision to lop off his lengthy locks came in response to an even more frustrating loss two nights earlier to the Spurs. He said afterward that the 48-point rout left him "f—ked up mentally." Kerr emphasized the importance of quickly moving on to the next one of 82.

After a 1-of-13 shooting performance Saturday night, Lendeborg came out tepid, passive and generally a mess on both sides of the ball. He turned the ball over as many times as he shot it in the first quarter and racked up a minus-25 differential by halftime in a 61-36 game.

Saddiq Bey #41 of the New Orleans Pelicans blocks the shot of Yaxel Lendeborg #1 of the Golden State Warriors in New Orleans, Monday, Nov. 16, 2026. Getty Images

He was visibly frustrated at times, just like two nights earlier, but coach Steve Kerr didn't think it was a product of Lendeborg's emotions bleeding over from the San Antonio loss.

"I thought he was frustrated because we were getting killed again," Kerr said. "He came over at one point and talked to me about some strategic stuff and made some really good adjustments at halftime and found his angles to attack."

Golden State Warriors forward Yaxel Lendeborg (1) dribbles against New Orleans Pelicans guard Jeremiah Fears (0) on Monday, Nov. 16, 2026, in New Orleans. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Lendeborg scored 20 on 7-of-14 shooting after halftime, but that was too little, too late.

He knew it. And so did the world. Because he showed as much.

"It's his first year, and we're putting an enormous responsibility on his shoulders," coach Steve Kerr said. "He has to go through this. There's no way around it. You can't cheat your way through that. You have to feel the highs and the lows. You heard his comments the other night. He cares about all this stuff, which is a great sign. But you have to learn how to manage that."

Golden State Warriors' Yaxel Lendeborg (1) plays against New Orleans Pelicans guard Jaron Pierre Jr. (10) in New Orleans, Monday, Nov. 16, 2026. AP Photo/Morgan Simmons

Green, who also doesn't often lack for words, kept it concise when asked about Lendeborg's night. Multiple times, he went up to the rookie with apparent words of advice.

"Just about how he's got to play with Steph not out there," he said.

Golden State Warriors forward Draymond Green (23) sits on the bench against the New Orleans Pelicans on Monday, Nov. 16, 2026, in New Orleans. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

It seemed to be a concerted effort on Green's part to tamp down the attention on Lendeborg, which he has rightfully earned with his Rookie-of-the-Year worthy play but also comes with certain expectations. The pressure only rises without Curry, his 30 points per game and gravity.

The spotlight is going to continue to follow him.

Internally, at least, the Warriors are in control of the dimmer switch.

"We can control what we can control," Green said. "We can't control the [outside] world."

#2772. Nvidia closes lower ahead of earnings as company reels from Huang resignation

Nvidia shares closed marginally lower Tuesday as former CEO Jensen Huang released his first public statement since his resignation and the company prepares to report earnings after the bell.

The stock move Tuesday marked Nvidia's fourth straight daily decline.

In the statement, Huang said he is open to returning as Nvidia's CEO and that he is committed to cooperating with the board's investigation into his behavior.

"I created problems for the Board that I deeply regret," Huang said in a statement obtained by CNBC's David Faber. "I own my mistakes completely, and I am committed to cooperating with the Board's independent investigation."

"If the Board of Directors decides that my return is in the best interest of our shareholders, our employees, the company, and America, I would be honored to answer these calls and return as CEO," Huang continued.

Huang resigned from Nvidia, the company's board announced on Thursday, following reports that he had a relationship with a woman U.S. intelligence reportedly believes to be a Chinese spy. Huang told employees he had an affair in an email on Nov. 8. Outlets also reported that Huang also had an affair with a vice president at the company and an "intimate relationship" with an unnamed second employee.

Nvidia's stock slid 4% on Monday, bringing shares to their lowest close since July 1, 2025.

Lawmakers also announced Tuesday that Huang will appear before the House Foreign Affairs, Intelligence, and China Select Committees on Dec. 1. Nvidia general counsel Tim Teter will appear before the same committees on Dec. 2.

Nvidia's stock slid 4% on Monday, bringing shares to their lowest close since July 1, 2025.

An online petition calling for Jensen Huang's reinstatement as Nvidia CEO drew more than 190,000 signatures, including Elon Musk. But Nvidia's board of directors is not considering bringing back Jensen Huang as CEO, a person familiar with the matter told CNBC on Monday.

The chip giant is also facing increasing scrutiny from U.S. lawmakers over Chinese companies' access to its advanced chips, which U.S. export controls restrict from going to China.

The U.S. Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry and Security, or BIS, announced on Monday an interim final rule designed to prevent Chinese companies' remote access to AI chips.

Nvidia announced on Tuesday morning that its board had established a committee responsible for oversight of the company's national security policies, export control compliance and global trade risk management.

The National Security and Export Control Oversight Committee will be chaired by director Suzanne Nora Johnson and will include directors Stephen C. Neal and John O. Dabiri as members, the company said in a statement.

#2771. Titans put Wan'Dale Robinson on injured reserve

The Titans have been happy with quarterback Cam Ward's recent play, but he'll have to keep it going without his most frequent target in the passing game.

Wide receiver Wan'Dale Robinson has been placed on injured reserve. Robinson injured his ankle during last Sunday's loss to the Jaguars.

Robinson had 38 catches for 272 yards and three touchdowns while being targeted 54 times this season. Rookie Carnell Tate has missed the team's last two games with a shoulder injury, but head coach Robert Saleh said on Monday that they hope the first-round pick can return in Week 11.

The Titans filled Robinson's roster spot by signing wide receiver Xavier Restrepo off of the practice squad. Restrepo was Ward's college teammate and he had three catches for 31 yards as a temporary elevation in Week 10.

They also announced that they have signed wide receiver Jamari Thrash to their practice squad.

#2770. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2768. Jensen Huang's Nvidia exit exposes succession and governance risks for companies and boards

To join the CNBC Workforce Executive Council, apply at cnbccouncils.com/wec.

The fallout from Jensen Huang's affair with an alleged Chinese spy as well as an Nvidia executive that led to his resignation as CEO of Nivida, the company he founded and grew into a $4 trillion chip giant, will likely go on for some time.

But the missteps of Nvidia's board of directors offer immediate lessons, say talent and leadership experts. It shows what happens when there's no clear succession plan, the dangers of an overly decentralized leadership structure, and how companies built around one powerful, controlling leader create the kind of risk boards are responsible for preventing.

In the span of less than a week — from the time Huang admitted that he was unfaithful to his wife until the board announced that he was resigning — the question became: could Nvidia, a company at the epicenter of the AI build-out, operate without him?

With Huang's ouster and the appointment of CFO Colette Kress as interim CEO, Nvidia's employees, customers, and investors will begin to find out. The process will also put a glaring spotlight on why the Nvidia board wasn't better prepared for Huang leaving the company for whatever reason.

"Succession planning is an ongoing, fundamental responsibility of the board, not something to do when a crisis hits," says Lisa Calicchio, chief human resources officer at WCG Clinical and a member of the CNBC Workforce Executive Council.

She described what happened at Nvidia as "a failure of epic proportion, both on the part of the board for not fulfilling its fiduciary responsibility to ensure continued viability of the organization by having a robust succession plan, and for the behavior and risks created by the CEO."

"What they're doing now is flying by the seat of their pants," she added.

Nvidia's 10-member board of directors is a collection of early investors, long-time partners and business executives across a range of industries. Before Nov. 12, the company did not have a chair of the board or any clear succession plan. The board also includes directors who expressed "confidence in Jensen" before they had even begun their own independent internal review of his actions.

In addition to naming Kress to the top job, the company appointed Harvey C. Jones, a tech investor and Nvidia director for the past 33 years, as its first-ever chair. After his appointment, the company announced that the board would be conducting a search of both internal and external candidates to find a permanent chief executive.

Alan Fleischmann, founder and CEO of business advisory firm Laurel Strategies and a member of the CNBC CEO Council, says that while "the DNA of any organization is defined by the CEO, there must be a stronger board dynamic and strong leadership surrounding the CEO."

"Succession planning is not simply about identifying who takes over when a CEO leaves," Fleischmann said. "It's about building an institution that can thrive beyond any one individual."

The outsized role Huang played at Nvidia — in September 2025 he told CNBC's Becky Quick that he had as many as 60 direct reports — makes it even more difficult for the board to find someone who can understand the inner workings of the company while being such a visible and active hype man for its all-important AI chips.

In corporate governance circles this is known as "key man" risk and it points to a company being overly reliant on one person whose death, departure, or financial or moral compromise could materially damage the organization.

"This is the world's most valuable company and all the risk centered on the shoulders of one man," Calicchio says.

"Flat organizations can be remarkably effective…but there are tradeoffs," Fleischmann added. "When too many decisions, relationships and reporting lines converge around one individual, the organization can become more vulnerable than it appears."

Calicchio acknowledged that a CEO having numerous direct reports isn't problematic in the early days of a company, when the founder knows everyone, but becomes concerning "when a company is the size of Nvidia. I really question the rigor and discipline of their organizational policies."

This same absence of rigor is evident in the lack of any obvious successors to Huang. "You would expect the board to have recognized at some point there was going to be an increasing need to look beyond Jensen," says Tim Wentworth, the former CEO of Walgreens Boots Alliance and Express Scripts. "This sounds so fundamental, but you don't wait for the rainy day to have the rainy day plan."

Wentworth says that when he was running both Walgreens and Express Scripts, he had a single board member assigned to each one of his direct reports so that "when we had board discussions, somebody in the room could speak about them and their strengths."

At WCG Clinical, succession and talent discussions are done quarterly, says Calicchio. "We're always looking at time frames," she says. "Is this person a definite successor, ready now, or is this someone you're grooming for the long term? Everyone wants to think that replacing the CEO is going to happen in an orderly way, but as we just saw at Nvidia, that's not always the case."

"Boards need to expect the unexpected," she adds.

Huang's powerful grip at Nvidia was great, "until it wasn't," says Wentworth. "It's really hard for a board to have a sense of urgency and the depth of intrusiveness necessary in that process when everything is going great with the company. No one wants to be the contrarian, but that's your job as a board member."

To join the CNBC Workforce Executive Council, apply at cnbccouncils.com/wec.

#2767. Eagles sign Jaydon Blue to the roster (once again), add practice squad DT

The Philadelphia Eagles officially announced two roster moves on Tuesday afternoon. Here's an overview:

RB Jaydon Blue was signed from the practice squad to the active roster.

DT Leki Fotu was signed to the practice squad.

Let's sort through this news on a player-by-player basis.

Here's a recap of Blue's transactions with the Eagles:

September 1 — Signed to the practice squad

October 22 — Signed to the practice squad

November 16 — Signed to the practice squad

The Eagles have been reluctant to play Blue on offense thus far; the former Dallas Cowboys draft pick has only logged eight offensive snaps this season. And all of those came during garbage time in the Eagles' loss to the Washington Commanders.

In addition to theoretically providing running back depth, Blue has been serving as the Eagles' kick returner. But he's only logging 22.3 yards per return, which isn't good.

It's a little curious why the Eagles signed Blue to their practice squad yesterday only to sign him to the roster one day later. Why not just sign him to the roster in the first place?

Regardless, Blue is back on the roster. Assuming Saquon Barkley suits up and Will Shipley returns from injured reserve, the Eagles will have Blue as their third running back option.

Until they inevitably cut him again down the road.

Fotu had a tryout with the Eagles on Monday. Here's what we wrote at the time:

The 28-year-old Fotu is a 6'5", 330 pound defensive tackle who's made 26 starts in 66 career games played from 2020-2025. With the Eagles trading Jordan Davis to the Jacksonville Jaguars, the Birds have a need for more nose tackle depth. Fotu can help to fill that role, be it on the practice squad or the roster.

Fotu is here to help fill practice reps and provide depth should the Eagles need him on game day.

#2766. LeBron Log No. 5: Popcorn in hand, a viral chorus and ‘basketball AI'

LeBron Log No. 5: Popcorn in hand, a viral chorus and 'basketball AI' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James spent Monday night in street clothes.

He still managed to argue a call with popcorn in his hand, practice his golf swing during a timeout and reject a report about himself before the Sixers left Brooklyn with a win.

That followed a Sunday afternoon in which James passed Jason Kidd on the NBA's career assists list and received an unusual description from Pacers head coach Rick Carlisle.

Welcome to the fifth edition of the LeBron Log.

James sat out the Sixers' 135-123 win over the Nets because of low back and sciatica management.

It was his fourth absence through 15 games. James has yet to play both ends of a back-to-back this season after logging nearly 33 minutes Sunday against Indiana.

He watched Monday's game from the end of the Sixers' bench in a yellow hat and an "Ohio Baby" sweatshirt. James snacked from a small bag before switching to popcorn.

When Joel Embiid believed he was fouled on a first-half drive, James stood and pleaded his case to official Jacyn Goble without putting down the box. He also joined conversations between teammates and officials during several timeouts.

James found another way to pass a third-quarter stoppage. As "Hot To Go" played inside Barclays Center, he practiced his golf swing near the bench.

His most pointed contribution came on Instagram.

An ESPN story by Dave McMenamin cited a Sixers source who said James had asked the coaching staff to remain patient while he worked toward finding his rhythm and conditioning by December.

James reposted ESPN's graphic and rejected the account.

"🧢! Those words never came out of my mouth!" he wrote.

The Sixers improved to 4-0 without James. They have beaten the Bucks, Hornets, Raptors and Nets in the games he has missed.

Brooklyn pulled within five points during the final five minutes Monday, putting the Sixers in another clutch game.

Philadelphia has reached 10 clutch wins more quickly than almost any team since the statistic began being tracked in 1996-97. The 2026-27 Sixers are the second team in that span with at least 10 such victories through their first 15 games.

The other was the 1997-98 Atlanta Hawks, who had 12.

James was also on the last team before Philadelphia to open a season with at least 10 consecutive clutch wins. The Lakers started 13-0 in those games last season.

James did not play in the Sixers' latest close finish, but he has now been part of the last two teams to start at least 10-0 under those circumstances.

Before Sunday's game, Carlisle explained why he tries to limit his play calls against James.

"LeBron James is one of the reasons we try not to have play calls, 'cause he knows all of them," Carlisle said. "He's a walking NBA basketball AI machine. That's what he is."

James followed with 15 points, 11 assists and seven rebounds in the Sixers' 131-124 win.

His pass to VJ Edgecombe for a layup with 1:19 remaining gave him 12,092 career assists and moved him past Kidd for third in NBA history. Only Chris Paul and John Stockton remain ahead of him.

Noah Levick wrote more about James' milestone and the anticipation behind his passes.

When told what Carlisle had called him, James appreciated the description.

"So, yeah, Rick is great," James said. "He's one of the greatest coaches of all time, and I've had my battles with him over the years. That's super humbling coming from a guy like that."

Philadelphia has found its soundtrack for James' biggest home moments.

The Sixers have played the viral "LeBron, LeBron, LeBron" song after several of his baskets, including his late putback against the Knicks on Oct. 30.

The refrain comes from a version of Steve Lacy's "Oh Yeah?" that replaces "Come on" with James' name. The Baylor University Men's Choir performed its own rendition, helping the song spread across social media.

Lacy later joined in. During an Oct. 15 concert at Freedom Mortgage Pavilion in Camden, he performed the LeBron version as the crowd sang along.

James has noticed Philadelphia's use of it.

"I've seen it. I've heard it. It's pretty hilarious, to be honest," James said. "I heard it towards the end of a game when we were winning. I made a play, and I heard that song. I was just chuckling to myself on the inside."

#2764. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2763. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2762. NFL makes surprising ‘Sunday Night Football’ change for Week 12 featuring unexpected teams

The Raiders and Browns will get primetime treatment in a couple of weeks.

In the first primetime flex of the 2026 NFL season, the league bumped the Patriots-Chargers matchup out of "Sunday Night Football," slotting Las Vegas' Week 12 visit to Cleveland into the coveted window on Nov. 29.

The Patriots and Chargers will instead kick off at 4:05 p.m. ET on CBS, while the Titans-Jaguars game, originally scheduled for that time slot, will move to 1 p.m. ET on Fox.

Las Vegas Raiders quarterback Fernando Mendoza (15) passes against the Seattle Seahawks during an NFL football game in Las Vegas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. AP Photo/John Locher

The move comes as New England's season appears to be teetering following a blowout loss to the Lions in Munich, Germany, on Sunday to fall to 5-5 on the year.

Patriots quarterback Drake Maye left that game during the first quarter after going down with an ankle injury, and it's still unclear when he will return to the field.

And although the Chargers got their first win of the season on Monday night, they have been the most disappointing team in football, sitting at 1-8.

The flex will give No. 1 overall pick Fernando Mendoza his first primetime start.

Cleveland Browns quarterback Deshaun Watson (4) celebrates a touchdown against the Houston Texans on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, in Cleveland. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

The Indiana product made his first career start Sunday following the trade of Kirk Cousins to Minnesota, throwing for 143 yards with a rushing touchdown during a loss to the Seahawks.

Cleveland, meanwhile, is surprisingly in playoff contention, giving the NFL a more compelling matchup to showcase in primetime.

The Browns head into their Week 11 bye at 6-4, having upset the Texans with Deshaun Watson's sixth game-winning drive of the season.

It will mark the Browns' first home game on "SNF" since 2019, while the Raiders had no primetime games on their schedule before the flex move.

#2761. HPU women sink Hampton, improve to 6-0

HIGH POINT — High Point University continued its strong start while Hampton struggled mightily.

The Panthers gained momentum offensively as the game went along, held the Pirates to single-digit scoring in every quarter, and won 81-21 in nonconference women's basketball Monday evening in the Qubein Center.

"I thought tonight was just a combination of we were at an elite level of execution defensively from a focus standpoint," High Point coach Wyatt Foust said. "Everybody that came in executed the defensive game plan great. They knew where we could take chances, where we didn't need to.

"It was a team effort to do a really good job on their best player, who's a first team all-conference level player in their league. And they just had a bad night. We're grateful we got to play a lot of guys. And just to see everybody celebrate each other, it's good to get everybody in there."

Meredith Tippner had 16 points, 11 rebounds and five assists to lead the Panthers (6-0). It was her third straight double-double.

"My coaches and my teammates have empowered me — that's got to be a role that I take for this team," Tippner said. "Part of it is like just go be a dog. I have people around me that are just like, 'You can do that.' I don't think I could be kind of that mentality without the people surrounding me. So it's been pretty cool."

Taylor Feldman added 13 points, eight rebounds and five assists. Juliann Woodard had 12 points and nine rebounds, while Rylee Bess had 11 points. Both Woodard and Bess made three 3s as High Point totaled nine for the game.

On offense, the Panthers, who tallied 17 assists, shot 48% from the field for the game — improving each quarter from 35% in the first to 63% in the fourth as their lead peaked at 61 in the final minute.

On defense, High Point held former Big South foe Hampton (1-4), led by Kennedy Hall with six points, to just 11.9% on 7-of-59 shooting — the lowest percentage and fewest made field goals by an opponent in program history.

The result was a 26-point advantage in the first half, a 33-10 lead at halftime and a 37-point difference in the second half (the second-largest margin in program history). The win was the 14th-largest in program history.

All 11 available players played — 10 scored while all 11 had at least one rebound. And High Point won for the sixth straight time to start the season for the first time in its Division I history.

The Panthers will play their next three on the road — Thursday at Vermont, plus Nov. 23 against Grand Canyon and Nov. 24 against Arkansas in the San Diego Classic. They will next play at home Nov. 29 against Army.

"They've worked so hard," Foust said. "I would put the work they've put in against any team in the entire country, so they deserve that recognition. They deserve to enjoy that and carry that through today. But we didn't come here to just be 6-0.

"We want to keep pressing and keep pushing. I think that's the expectation. And I think that we're going to win them all. We are excited and we'll celebrate today and then we'll turn the page and get ready to go. We've got a really, really tough Vermont team at their place coming up."

#2760. Kalshi takes down controversial Trump 'mention' markets at CFTC's request

Prediction market platform Kalshi quietly paused trading in a handful of "mention" contracts tied to President Donald Trump at the end of last week at the request of the CFTC, the company confirmed on Tuesday.

Mention markets are made up of contracts that ask traders what specific words will be used in a speech, a corporate earnings call or during a television broadcast.  Earlier this year, a White House teleprompter operator used inside information to place trades in mention markets related to the president's comments.

Markets paused include those asking traders what nicknames Trump will say before 2027, what the president will say in November and what countries and companies Trump will say before the end of the month.

The move comes after the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, the federal agency overseeing prediction markets, said in a letter to exchanges in September that mentions contracts have a greater manipulation risk.

A spokesperson for the agency told CNBC that the commission has placed holds on the certification of some of Kalshi's proposed mention markets. The spokesperson cited a Kalshi contract on hold that asked what key words would Trump say before a certain date. The contract was originally self-certified by Kalshi in February 2025.

"The CFTC stayed the certification of these contracts because they may be susceptible to manipulation, which is consistent with the staff advisory the CFTC put out two months ago," said Brooke Nethercott, the agency's spokesperson.

In a statement, Kalshi spokesperson Dani Lever said Kalshi is complying with the order.

In August, reports emerged that the CFTC was conducting an internal review into mention markets. The agency also requested that Kalshi, which is the only U.S.-regulated exchange that features the contract type, take down its sports-related mention markets in response to the inquiry. Kalshi complied.

A Trump-related mention market drew scrutiny in July after it was revealed a White House teleprompter operator placed trades on markets related to the president's comments. In a settlement with the operator, the CFTC ordered a three-year trading ban on the individual and a penalty amounting to over $172,000.

Kalshi has defended mentions markets even as the CFTC has become increasingly skeptical of them, saying that the individual words of prominent people often cause billions of dollars of movement across financial markets.

In addition to scrutiny regarding mention markets, the company is also facing a probe from the CFTC about its communications about new products with the agency, though a spokesperson for the company told CNBC that it was working to resolve the issues.

As of Tuesday morning, a mention market asking what Vice President JD Vance would say during the New York Times DealBook Summit event on Wednesday is still active.

Disclosure: CNBC and Kalshi have a commercial relationship that includes customer acquisition and a minority investment.

#2757. WNBA interviewing commissioner candidates as Cathy Engelbert’s tenure winds down

Outgoing WNBA Commissioner Cathy Engelbert presided over her final league Board of Governors meeting Monday in a Midtown boardroom as the search for her replacement ramps up.

Representatives from all 15 teams were in attendance. Several new faces were in the room, too, including representatives from the three expansion franchises — Cleveland, Detroit and Philadelphia — that will join the league over the next four years.

NBA Commissioner Adam Silver also stopped in for the final hour.

WNBA Commissioner Cathy Engelbert speaks following Game 7 of the WNBA Finals at Chase Center in San Francisco on Saturday, Oct. 31, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

The group voted on some items from last week's competition committee meeting, though Engelbert didn't share any details. She said points of emphasis for officiating next season won't be determined until April.

But the biggest question looming over this one-day summit is who will stand at the front of the room next year.

Engelbert, the league's first commissioner who oversaw historic expansion and the WNBA's jump to mainstream popularity, announced in September that she planned to retire at the end of the year.

The search for Engelbert's replacement, led by Excel Search & Advisory, is in full swing, with more than a dozen candidates under consideration.

Alex Rodriguez, co-owner of the Minnesota Timberwolves and Lynx, said the Board of Governors discussed what traits they'd like to see in the next WNBA commissioner.

WNBA Commissioner Cathy Engelbert speaks with the media before Game 1 of the WNBA Playoff Finals at Chase Center in San Francisco on Oct. 17, 2026. Icon Sportswire via Getty Images

"Someone who has marketing experience, business, someone who's a very good leader, someone who loves basketball and loves players," the former Yankees star said. "As a former player, I always had a great relationship with [MLB Commissioner] Rob Manfred, and I still do, and I think that's really important."

Engelbert said they're "well past" the exploratory stage of the search process and have started interviews.

"The nice thing is the league's on the map, so we have a lot of interest from a lot of different candidates with broad capabilities and backgrounds," Engelbert said. "I'm really excited for who's going to take over next."

Engelbert said she'll join the interview process once the list is trimmed down. A committee of NBA and WNBA owners will also be part of the hiring process.

NBA Commissioner Adam Silver speaks to the media before an NBA Cup basketball game between the San Antonio Spurs and the New York Knicks at T-Mobile Arena in Las Vegas, Tuesday, Dec. 16, 2025. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

Silver declined to share who's on the committee. Rodriguez confirmed he's not, though.

If Engelbert's successor isn't identified in the next 1 ½ months, Silver could tap Deputy Commissioner Mark Tatum to take over on an interim basis. Tatum previously served as the interim WNBA president between Lisa Borders' exit in October 2018 and Engelbert's hiring in July 2019.

Engelbert also believes the WNBA could explore adding its own Deputy Commissioner in the near future.

"That's something, probably in the next couple years, would probably make sense," she said.

#2756. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2755. Trump on Iran's reported DC dirty bomb plan: 'We take it very seriously'

President Donald Trump said Tuesday that if a report about Iran developing plans to target top administration officials with a "dirty bomb" in Washington is true, Iran would face consequences.

"Well, if there is, they will pay a price the likes of which no country has ever paid before," Trump said when asked if there is any imminent threat to Washington, home to close to 700,000 people in addition to the seat of government.

"I hope it's not, but we take it very seriously, because they happen to be crazy," Trump said during a photo op with world leaders at the summit of the Association of Southeast Asian Nations in Manila, Philippines.

"This is why they can't have a nuclear weapon, by the way, in case people had any questions," Trump added, reiterating his main rationale for launching a war with Iran in late February.

Late Monday, CNN cited two U.S. officials in reporting that new U.S. intelligence has intercepted "very specific" plans, including "locations and targets," related to a potential Iranian plot to set off a dirty bomb, which combines conventional explosives with radioactive material.

Officials have debated whether Iran would actually attempt to detonate such a device in the U.S., or even if it could, CNN reported, citing seven U.S. officials in total.

CNBC has not verified the report. Trump administration spokeswoman Lauren Bis told CNN in a statement that the U.S. is "monitoring any and all potential threats, but at this time there are no credible threats to the homeland."

Trump was asked about imminent Iranian threats during the first leg of his tour through Asia, which began at ASEAN and is set to include high-stakes meetings with Chinese President Xi Jinping and Russian President Vladimir Putin.

Asked about the dirty bomb report on CNBC's "Squawk Box" Tuesday morning, Homeland Security Secretary Markwayne Mullin suggested the Iranian plot was not new and described Tehran as "aspirational" in its threats.

If Iran were to take such an action, "you would cause an all-out war" with the U.S., which would attack at such a level that the Islamic Republic's allies, such as China and Russia, would not come to its aid, Mullin said.

It is a threat, and "we take it very serious," he said. "But the homeland is secure, I can tell you that. But every day we addressing new threats."

CNN reported later Tuesday morning that NSA Net, the classified communications network used by the National Security Agency, is down, citing six people familiar with the matter.

#2754. James reaches assists milestone, keeps seeing plays ‘before they actually happen'

James reaches assists milestone, keeps seeing plays 'before they actually happen' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Tyrese Maxey admitted that he wasn't entirely ready for LeBron James' first pass of the afternoon Sunday.

Maxey cut through the paint and James' bounce pass was perfect, but the Sixers' All-NBA guard missed his layup. He's not fully accustomed yet to James' precognitive gifts.

"There's still some times when you're surprised," Maxey said after the Sixers' win over the Pacers. "… I was cutting through just to cut and create space. The ball hit me in the chest and I missed the layup. I was like, 'Ah shoot, my bad.' He was like, 'Yeah, you weren't looking.'"

James has been trying to teach his teammates that he'll spot openings they may not notice.

"I just tell my guys, 'Always pay attention to me when I've got the ball,' because I definitely see plays develop before they happen," James said.

For the most part, James' teammates adhered to his request on the latest historic day in a milestone-filled career. By setting up VJ Edgecombe's layup with 1:19 left in the fourth quarter, James moved into third place on the NBA's all-time assists list, passing Jason Kidd.

The 22-time All-Star recorded 11 assists, his most so far as a Sixer, and now sits at 12,092 in his career. Chris Paul (12,552 assists) and John Stockton (15,806) are the only two players above James.

"It's obviously super humbling," James said. "It's something that I didn't foresee in my career, but I knew assists meant a lot to me. Just the movement of the ball, being able to find my teammates, putting the ball on time and on target.

"And also just being linked with some of the greats that have ever played this game and a dear friend of mine, J-Kidd. It's really special."

James is at or near the top of many all-time leaderboards. He's first in NBA history in points, games and minutes, sixth in steals and fifth in triple-doubles.

Passing has always been a core element of his game.

"First of all, he's got the vision and the awareness," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "He's got the size to see. I think he's got some anticipation. Just a half-second before a play happens, he knows how the defense is moving. … And he throws crisp passes. Some of those are cooking out to the corner three guys, so it doesn't let the close-out get there nearly as quickly."

Whether it's a long-range rocket, a drive-and-kick dish or a cross-court feed from the post that suggests he might have eyes in the back of his head, James has always enjoyed finding a wide range of ways to help his teammates.

"For someone that talented, he's very unselfish," Maxey said. "He really prides himself on getting guys open."

As James recalled Sunday, he had exceptional passing tools long before he entered the NBA 24 seasons ago.

"Since I was a kid, I've had the ability to see plays before they actually happen," he said. "Seeing movement, seeing cracks in the defense before they were even open … it's something that's been with me since I was a kid.

"I just had that vision and it just stuck with me throughout my playing days."

#2752. David Bednar Accepts New York Yankees’ $23.15 Million Qualifying Offer

#2751. Aschenbrenner’s Situational Awareness made AI bets again before October rout

Leopold Aschenbrenner's artificial intelligence-themed hedge fund Situational Awareness, which nearly collapsed in July, loaded back up on AI stocks as it returned to the financial markets at the end of the third quarter. But all of its holdings are down big the last month and a half.

According to a new regulatory filing, as the fund got back on its feet Aschenbrenner took an equity position in Australian neocloud company SharonAI valued at more than $440 million, along with equity stakes in AI-centric plays Cleanspark, IREN, Bitdeer Technologies and Fermi. The Cleanspark stake is worth almost $39 million, while Iren and Bitdeer stakes are more than $21 million and more than $20 million, respectively. Fermi, meanwhile, has a value of more than $11 million.

The fund also owned call options at the end of September in neocloud Nebius, memory chip name Sandisk, the Roundhill Memory ETF (DRAM) and data center power play Bloom Energy. Sandisk and Nebius were key holdings that almost took out his fund over the summer.

All of these Situational Awareness holdings have been routed since Sept. 30. SharonAI is at the top of that list, seeing a 58% loss in October. It's also fallen 11% so far this month. IREN was down 41% in October, while Nebius declined 35%. Last week, Nebius plummeted more than 16% in a single day after the company issued a weak full-year forecast.

It's not known whether the hedge fund manager got out of those positions during the October AI selloff. The 13F filing is just a snapshot of what the hedge fund held at the end of the quarter.

Aschenbrenner — who launched the fund in 2024 after rising to fame that same year with his AI manifesto also entitled Situational Awareness — re-entered financial markets a bit in September, sources told CNBC at the time.

The Monday filing shows he picked up right where he left off.

The near-collapse of his hedge fund over the summer forced him to sell all of its public equity positions to Ken Griffin's Citadel. The fund had $45 billion in assets at its peak, but that tumbled to around $10 billion, consisting mostly of private stakes.

Stocked tethered to the AI trade took a hit in the most recent quarter as investors grew concerned about a slowdown in development of the technology. Those fears were spurred by comments from industry leaders such as Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei and OpenAI CEO Sam Altman back in September, with the latter saying that the company going public this year would be "ill-advised."

Since then, pressure on the trade has only continued.

In recent weeks, investors have worried about how Democratic wins in the midterm elections could hinder data center development, whether some technology companies are using too much debt to finance the AI buildout and what role Nvidia will play in that financing with Jensen Huang now out as CEO at the company.

Losses in Nvidia as well as other AI-related stocks dragged down the three leading U.S. indexes during Monday's session as investors continued to assess the fallout of Huang's departure.

#2750. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2749. Paul George flips the script — and the bird — in Philadelphia return

Paul George flips the script — and the bird — in Philadelphia return originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

PG-13 was rated R in his return to Philadelphia.

The middle finger Paul George directed toward one baseline fan only reinforced that rating.

Tuesday night was expected to revolve around Jaylen Brown and Jayson Tatum sharing an NBA court as opponents for the first time. Instead, the other headliner in the summer's blockbuster trade stole the show.

George scored a game-high 36 points in Boston's 123-101 win over the Sixers. He shot 14 for 22 from the floor, made six of his 11 three-point attempts and scored 14 consecutive Boston points in the fourth quarter.

The game was tied at 79 with 3:19 remaining in the third quarter. Boston outscored Philadelphia 44-22 from that point forward.

The Celtics went nine minutes and 20 seconds without making a two-point field goal during the second half. Instead, they made nine consecutive field goals from three, the second-longest such streak by a Sixers opponent over the last 30 seasons.

Payton Pritchard opened the fourth with consecutive threes. George followed with eight straight points, helping the Celtics begin the quarter on a 14-0 run that turned a competitive game into a rout.

George's return carried a complicated history. His two seasons with the Sixers were repeatedly interrupted by injuries, but he also played a significant role in their most memorable playoff run in years.

Last spring, George helped the Sixers overcome a 3-1 deficit to eliminate Boston in seven games. He averaged 17.4 points and shot 55 percent from three during the series.

Tyrese Maxey was happy to see his former teammate Tuesday, even if he disliked what followed.

"It was good to see Paul," Maxey said. "I love him, love his family. … He gave us everything he had while he was here. We know what he did in the playoffs last year. Great person, great family, and we'll see him again."

Maxey then added one important qualifier.

George instead scored from nearly everywhere.

"He had some open threes. He had some pull-up threes. He had some middies," Maxey said. "He played within the game, too. That's what Paul does. He plays within the game. He takes what the defense gives him, and we gave him too many open looks tonight.

"PG, we know what PG does. We shouldn't even be allowing him to get up 11 threes, and that's on us."

Nick Nurse watched George attack the Sixers in several ways.

"He really had it going," Nurse said. "He'd post up, and-one, fallaways. He went down the lane, high archer off the glass, and-one. He scored in about every way he could tonight."

George heard boos during his return and said the arena carried the atmosphere, though not quite the intensity, of a playoff game.

"This building can be very electric as well," George said. "It was fun. It was fun to play in this environment."

One interaction extended beyond the usual reception for a returning player.

During a stoppage, the ball rolled toward a fan seated along the baseline. George said the fan looked directly at him before dropping the ball instead of returning it.

"There was a fan being an a–," George said. "The ball rolled to him. He looked me dead in my eyes, dropped the ball on the ground as if I'm a dog to go fetch or go get it, instead of passing the ball to me like a normal fan would do.

"And so, yeah, then I rolled him a middle finger in return."
