#2695. Warriors changes coming after second straight blowout loss without Steph Curry

NEW ORLEANS — When you lose in blowout fashion to a red-hot team that was in the NBA Finals five months ago, that's one thing. When it's followed by an almost-as-disastrous defeat to a bottom-of-the-barrel squad that had dropped its past six games, something's got to give.

"Especially in the fashion that we've lost," guard De'Anthony Melton said, "expect things to change. As they should, given the last two losses, how we lost."

Golden State Warriors head coach Steve Kerr looks on during an NBA basketball game against the New Orleans Pelicans in New Orleans, Monday, Nov. 16, 2026. AP Photo/Morgan Simmons

And changes are afoot for the Warriors after their lousy 115-94 loss to the Pelicans on Monday.

"We tried to change it up from San Antonio, change the starting lineup, but [it] just didn't seem to have a lot of juice," coach Steve Kerr said. "We're gonna have to change some combinations and find some things that work for us."

The task is easier said than done with Stephen Curry out until at least Thanksgiving.

Without Curry, it's pretty much back to the drawing board for Kerr and his staff. Throw out the window all the lineups that produced the NBA's second-best offensive rating and an eight-game winning streak before Curry tore a muscle in his oblique last week in Houston.

"I've got to do my job as a coach to find the right starting group, to make sure we have good five-man combinations coming off the bench," Kerr said. "It's kind of the early part of the season where you're experimenting, you're looking at different combinations — that sort of thing. That's our job as a staff, and it's the players' job to find that energy and that juice."

In each of their past three losses, the Warriors have fallen behind by double digits in the first quarter. They lost Curry five minutes into their first game of this road trip against the Rockets and fell behind by 15 early. They allowed the Spurs to open an 18-0 lead en route to a 38-14 advantage after the first quarter on Saturday.

And on Monday, they converted just two attempts from the field on 18 tries while allowing the lowly Pelicans to build a 35-11 first-quarter lead.

"It felt like we had no force, no energy," Kerr said. "Without Steph, we've got to mix and match a little bit. We'll have to experiment with some different lineups.

"But I'm looking for the force of cutting and playing with a sense of urgency, the connection that comes when the ball starts to move off of those cuts — we need much more of that."

Golden State Warriors guard De'Anthony Melton dribbles during an NBA basketball game against the New Orleans Pelicans in New Orleans, Monday, Nov. 16, 2026. AP Photo/Morgan Simmons

Kerr said he would take a look at the Warriors' best two-man combos from last season. By net rating, that would mean more time together on the floor for Brandin Podziemski and Melton (plus-6.1 net rating), Podziemski and Al Horford (plus-4.1), as well as Melton and Gui Santos (plus-2.5).

But here's the Warriors' harsh reality: Of the 13 two-man combos that produced positive net ratings last season, 10 of them are unavailable due to injuries.

Kerr will also study the tape, as he did after the Warriors' 48-point loss to the Spurs on Saturday. He applied what he saw on film and adjusted the starting lineup, subbing out Melton in favor of two-way point guard and 3-point marksman LJ Cryer. That wasn't the answer, either.

Golden State Warriors dribbler LJ Cryer (18) controls the ball against the New Orleans Pelicans in New Orleans, Monday, Nov. 16, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

"One of the things that I saw in San Antonio was [that] Draymond [Green] needed LJ's shooting with him in the pick-and-roll game," Kerr said. "That's why we started LJ. Draymond got him a couple great looks early that didn't go. It's going to happen. It's just one of those nights for LJ."

The Warriors can only afford to have so many more of those nights. The margin for error was already thin waiting on the returns of Jimmy Butler and Moses Moody in January or February.

Now, without Curry, the heat is on Kerr to find a way to win a game. Just one would be a start.

"Without Steph, obviously, we're looking for different combinations that are going to click," he said. "We've got to find combos that provide the shooting and the spacing that we need — the cutting — it's early in the season, so we've got to experiment as well."

#2694. Tuesday Miami Dolphins Thoughts With The Earl

This game really depends upon your outlook for the season. If you are a "get the first draft pick at all costs" person, then you like this outcome. If you want to see a win and positive development, this was a horrible outcome. It was another of the "close, but no cigar" games the Dolphins have played this season. Here's what I saw.

In previous games, there was a tradeoff between the offense and defense over which would show up. In this game, the offense was expected to be worse than the defense, with a backup QB getting his first-ever NFL action. However, the Dolphins still couldn't go a game without some form of tradeoff. In this matchup, it was a tradeoff between the pass defense and the run defense. The Dolphins allowed a completion percentage over 70% and had a run defense that allowed less than 4 yards per carry. The pass defense had its best performance of the season. They held Danny Dimes to 50% completion and a 4.9 YPA average. They picked him off twice, one of which was returned for a TD. They allowed a passing TD and a couple of big plays but were solid in coverage. In a shock to everyone, the Dolphins allowed more rushing yards to TE Tyler Warren (3) than receiving yards (1). Warren was held without a catch until the 4th quarter and was a complete non-factor.

Unfortunately, while the Dolphins pass defense stepped up covering the one receiving threat the Colts had, the run defense couldn't stop the other threat the Colts posed. The Colts rushed for 172 yards on 34 carries, 106 of those yards coming in the 1st half on 19 carries. That's a 5.6 YPA allowed. They improved a little in the second half, allowing 66 yards on 15 carries and a 4.4 YPA. But on the final drive, they allowed 12 yards on 2 carries that finished off the game. Just once, the team needs a complete performance on both sides of the ball. Now they need a complete performance on both sides of the ball. I will give them some due credit. They limited the Colts to 17 offensive points and were hamstrung by a limited offense, but the run defense was the backbreaker in this game. With the injuries mounting on the DL, I don't know how they can fix it.

We'll get to the obvious problem in a moment. Bradley Pinion had 3 punts for 134 yards, with a 55-yarder. That's an average of 44.7 overall and 39.5 on the other two punts. Indy's punter had 6 for 290, averaging 48.3. The league average is 47.5. Miami still allowed too many yards in the kick return game. They had the 15-yard penalty on Louis, though I won't complain about penalties since the total was lopsided in Miami's favor and that penalty was ultimately inconsequential. It is still unacceptable, though.

I said on Friday that the Dolphins simply aren't good enough to win without winning in the margins. This is a young team working to find its identity. They come to work every day and put it all on the line every game. This isn't the 2024 or 2025 Dolphins with players obviously checking out and Tua laughing on the sideline or Hill taking himself out of a game. This is a bad team playing like they are trying to get the top seed in the AFC. When the kicker, the second oldest player on the team, shanks not one, but two FGs and misses a PAT as the icing on the cake, that is a level of terrible that cannot be measured. After I watched the game and went to bed thinking of all I planned to say about how he cannot remain on the team if the coaches wanna keep the locker room, the Dolphins made my job easier and waived him on Monday. So that is a non-issue now.

And I've seen the tweets about how the misses keep the tank alive and all that. But I cannot stress enough how not only is tanking a bad idea in general, but you really only tank for a QB, and the 2027 QB class is buns. They are bad. It's an elevated class due to absences and wouldn't be considered top prospects in a class like 2018 or 2020. I would love for the Dolphins to get Jeremiah Smith as much as any other fan. But this team is NOT trying to lose. They aren't playing like they are trying to lose. They are talent deficient, but they aren't tanking. So when you play as hard as they do and the veteran kicker can't do the basic requirement of putting the ball through the uprights, then a message needs to be sent, and it was.

Kyle McCord's play will be dissected by many others, so I don't want to get deep into it. I thought he played ok. I thought he played like a player getting his first NFL snaps. His performance isn't worth getting worked up over one way or another. I said in Friday's post that a healthy Willis wins this game by 2 scores, and I still believe it. But McCord played well enough for the Dolphins to get a win against another equally terrible team and was let down. The next step will be to see what, if any, level of improvements can be made.

The run game has disappeared, and it probably won't come back for a while. I tried to watch to see how much Indy loaded the box, but I didn't truly keep track. Indy is a bad team, but Miami will face some good teams coming up; teams with solid to great defenses. My guess is they will load the box and force McCord to beat them. Having Meyer in there instead of Brewer hurt as well, but the OL can only do so much. The good news for the RBs is they were involved in the passing game. All three RBs were targeted 9 times with 8 catches for 62 yards, including the 4-yard loss for Kaytron Allen. If the RBs can contribute in the pass game, then the offense can function about as normally as we can expect. Even with the drop-off, Gordon is still on pace for a 1000 yard season.

Caleb Douglas was invisible for most of the game but stepped up late. He will continue to be a force. Washington had the play of the game IMO with the one-handed catch down the sideline. He continues to improve in getting separation and being a factor in the offense. The TD throw was nice also.

The TEs were invisible, and that's a concern. I like Dulcich, and he has potential, but he is playing his way off the roster for 2027.

The passing defense stepped up as I mentioned, and its leaders are two rookies. Michael Taaffe logged his 3rd INT this season, and Chris Johnson got his 2nd in consecutive weeks. It should have been his 3rd in 4 weeks, but that Jets WR fell down and they said Johnson interfered. He was a ballhawk in college, and it is showing up now. There are 17 players in the league with 3 or more INTs and only 7 with more than 3. Taaffe has the instincts to be around the ball a lot. His development going into the rest of this season will be interesting to watch.

Regarding the kicker position, I would not be opposed to the Dolphins adding a young player – an undrafted rookie or second-year player – to fill the position. Vets are what they are, and while they offer some reliability, I'm all for giving a young player a chance to make his mark. They probably won't because, well, they are trying to win games. But if they swing and miss with young players, at least they tried. If they get a hit, that's something they can build on moving forward.

Circling back to the opening paragraph, I am not a fan of losing games for a draft pick. I want the Dolphins to win. This game was frustrating because they had it and gave it away. They lost by the margins and lost the game. Unfortunately, this was the last win on the schedule for a while. Unless McCord magically turns into Tom Brady in the next few games, the Dolphins are looking at 1-16. They host the Jets in 2 weeks, but I don't like that matchup with McCord. They host the hapless Chargers in week 16, and if Willis is back healthy, they'll have a good chance of winning. They have the 8-1 Bills and 7-2 Broncos on the road in 2 of the next 3 games. They get the 7-2 Bears and 6-4 Packers, all battling for playoff seeding and that division title. Perhaps the Bills will lock up home-field advantage by week 17, and the Dolphins will get backups. Otherwise, the outlook is grim. To paraphrase Captain Jack Sparrow: if you were waiting for the opportune moment to get a win… that was it.

We all knew this season wasn't about the wins, but 1-16 is a tough mark for anyone. Even if the Dolphins go 12-5 over the next 2 seasons (they won't), that is still 25-26 over 3 seasons. It's going to be much worse than that. It is going to be a long road back to relevance for this franchise. I'll cover that later on (either this week or next), so stay tuned.

#2693. Dodgers Cut Ties With 2 Players Amid Roster Crunch

The Los Angeles Dodgers on Tuesday announced that two players will be designated for assignment as they work to finalize their 40-man roster.

The Dodgers said that right-handed pitcher Jake Cousins and infielder Christian Cairo are being DFA'd, while right-handed pitcher Christian Zazueta and lefties Jackson Ferris, Adam Serwinowski and Luke Fox are being added to the 40-man roster.

It's a move that will protect Zazueta, Ferris, Serwinowski and Fox from the upcoming Rule 5 Draft.

That event is scheduled for Dec. 9, but, of course, the looming lockout could prompt changes in that plan.

Cousins and Cairo now could be part of a trade, picked up on waivers by another team, sent down to the minors or released.

For Cairo, a return to the minors seems highly likely, as he's spent all seven of his professional seasons there.

Cousins, though, has played 88 games in the majors over four seasons.

He's returning from Tommy John surgery and hasn't played in MLB since 2024, but it's possible that he gets another shot in the big leagues.

Oct 28, 2024; New York, New York, USA; New York Yankees pitcher Jake Cousins (61) throws during the sixth inning against the Los Angeles Dodgers in game three of the 2024 MLB World Series at Yankee Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Wendell Cruz-Imagn Images

In his four seasons — three with the Milwaukee Brewers and one with the New York Yankees — he has a career ERA of 2.78 and WHIP of 1.246, with a strikeout to walk ratio of 2.19.

In protecting Zazueta, Ferris, Serwinowski and Fox, the Dodgers have invested in their pitching staff.

Zazueta, the team's No. 5 overall prospect and top pitching prospect, was a no-brainer to add to the 40-man roster, and Ferris and Serwinowski are likely top-20 prospects as well.

Feb 22, 2026; Peoria, Arizona, USA; Los Angeles Dodgers pitcher Jackson Ferris against the San Diego Padres during a spring training game at Peoria Sports Complex. Mandatory Credit: Mark J. Rebilas-Imagn Images

The real question revolves around Fox, who isn't in the Dodgers' top 40 prospects but who might now get his first taste of the big leagues in 2027.

For the Tulsa Drillers of Double-A in 2026, he racked up a 3.29 ERA in nine games, including eight starts.

He had a WHIP of 0.91 and had 35 strikeouts against 15 hits.

Are you sad to see Cousins and Cairo go, and which of the young pitchers are you most excited for?

#2692. Chargers' win puts Titans in the driver's seat for the first pick in the 2027 NFL draft

There are no winless teams in the NFL after the Chargers won on Monday night. Which might be good news for the Titans.

There’s now a three-way tie for the worst record in the NFL, with the Titans, Chargers and Dolphins all at 1-8. The draft order tiebreaker goes to the team with the weakest strength of schedule, and that gives the Titans an edge.

The Titans’ 17 opponents this season currently have a cumulative winning percentage of .481, which gives them a big edge over the Chargers (whose strength of schedule is.528) and Dolphins (.588). The Titans also have a weaker strength of schedule than the 2-8 Jets and 2-7 Eagles, who are next in the draft order. If there’s a tie for the first pick, the Titans are likely to win it.

If the Titans end up with the first overall pick, the big question will be whether they believe in quarterback Cam Ward enough to use the first pick to build around him, or whether they think it’s time to draft a quarterback to replace Ward. The current betting favorite to be the first overall pick is Ohio State wide receiver Jeremiah Smith. Drafting Smith would be a strong statement that the Titans are committed to putting the right players around Ward. After the Chargers’ loss on Monday, the Titans are in good shape to get that opportunity.

#2691. NBA Game Preview: Timberwolves vs Blazers Thread

Well hello there, Blazers. Good to see you again. The Wolves welcome back the team from the Rose City on the second night of a double-header bout. In the "Brains of the Operation Bowl" between Wolves head coach Chris Finch and former Wolves assistant coach, now Blazer's head coach Micah Nori, Finch holds the lead with Micah looking to close the gap.

For more on that game, go and check out Troy Asseln's marvelous piece.

I have been thoroughly impressed with Nori's work thus far in coaching this Blazers team up to a top 10 offense. Damian Lillard looks like the Dame of old. They have not minimized Deni Avdija though how he's able to get the whistle he does while the Wolves are begging for a call is beyond me. And though currently injured, even Ja Morant is looking primed for redemption in this new system.

The Blazers will be looking for redemption of their own in Target Center tonight, lets see how the Wolves can rain on their parade.

Who: Minnesota Timberwolves (10-4) vs. Portland Trailblazers (9-4)

When: Tuesday, November 17th at 7:00 PM CT

Local TV: DAZN-MN, KATU, KUNP, BlazerVision

These back-to-back matchups against the same team thing has been around for a bit now. Since 2021, the Wolves and Blazers have played in two such matchups. The first they drew in 2021. The second in 2024, Portland pantsed the Wolves twice. With one game over in this series, Minnesota can even the score.

As simple as it sounds, a thing that is very different from most regular season play where a team is preparing for a whole new opponent in their next matchup, these same team back-to-backs allow for more of a playoff atmosphere. Counter punches are planned.

Basic schemes that were scouted, film that was watched, all of the preparation has now been tested. As such, each team has a better sense of how to take away the game plan of their familiar opponent.

More than that, there is pride on the line. No one likes losing. At the board game table or at the park basketball courts, a new game is called for by the losing side to avenge the defeat. This is a rare moment where it happens in professional basketball.

The Blazers are one of the most efficient shooting teams in basketball, ranking ninth overall in field goal percentage (46.3%). They take the fourth most three-point attempts (43.4) and third most free throw attempts (29.5), the latter stat being mostly due to Avdija.

Last night the Wolves kept the foul sheet mostly clean with 21 fouls to the Blazer's 28. Portland attempted seven more threes, but made only two more than Minnesota. Meanwhile the Wolves strong rebounding continued as the swallowed up 70 boards. No one could hit a consistent shot last night save for Terrance Shannon Jr., Donovan Clingan, and Avdija.

Both teams shot under 40% from the field and under 30% from three. This is to the Wolves advantage to junk up the game. In a hopefully more common occurrence, Minnesota shot over 70% from the free throw line to the tune of 30 extra points, almost half of them from TJ.

The Wolves played this game sans Anthony Edwards, Donte DiVincenzo and Jaylen Clark for only his second missed game of the season, this time on account of a sore hamstring. Towards the end of the game just before the starters were pulled, Jonathan Kuminga fell down and took an accidental knee in the back from a Blazers player, sustaining a shoulder injury. Minnesota had to call a timeout as the medical staff gently helped him to the bench before he exited the game into the locker room.

This afternoon, the Wolves announced he would be out due to a right shoulder contusion. Hopefully this is just a short-term absence and utilizing caution on the end of a back-to-back.

One the great storylines through this season has been Kuminga taking on primary point of attack defensive duties allowing Jaden McDaniels to roam. The painted area will still be patrolled by "Walking Top 10 Defense" Rudy Gobert, but with McDaniels more zeroed in on someone like Avdija, Portland will be able to puncture the Wolves shell and put the defense in the blender.

Championships and basketball immortality are now built on endurance and the nicks and bumps sustained during the regular season echo out all the louder when teams are asked to suit back up less than 24 hours from their last game. Those Wolves healthy enough to take the court need to rub some dirt in, and hold the line for much needed reinforcements.

The Timberwolves continue to junk things up, pressing on Avdija just far enough off his preferred spot without allowing him to hear the chirp chirp of the whistle he loves so dearly. The game stays knotted through the first half and the Wolves unlock their offense while locking up the Blazers in the second. TJ blazes trails of his own all the way to the cup for enough three point plays the old fashioned way that a bunch of hipsters start ordering Terrance Shannon's at the Loon and the barkeep gives back a knowing smile as they grab the rye, syrup, and bitters.

Our blogging buddies are over at Blazer's Edge. Be kind to our blazing buddies, the last thing we need between friends is a wedge.

#2688. Former Kyler Murray backup reveals why he didn’t live up to Vikings’ expectations

Former Kyler Murray backup reveals why he didn't live up to Vikings' expectations appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Minnesota Vikings' experiment with Kyler Murray has not produced the resurgence former teammate Brett Hundley expected, and the former quarterback believes Kevin O'Connell's offense has played a role.

Hundley, who backed up Murray with the Arizona Cardinals during his rookie season in 2019, recently told Action Network's Kyle Odegard that Minnesota's timing-based offense has not been a natural fit for Murray's skill set.

"That offense is built around timing. If you threw Brock Purdy in there, he'd be a great fit for that offense. That's the type of quarterback. I think Kyler is a great quarterback, but it's just not a true fit. When he was drafted in Arizona under Kliff Kingsbury, the offense was built around him, and that's where Kyler had his best years. We haven't seen that (in Minnesota)," Hundley said.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Murray has started six games for Minnesota this season, completing 100 of 154 passes for 1,022 yards, five touchdowns and three interceptions. He owns an 86.6 passer rating and has added 167 rushing yards on 29 attempts. Minnesota went 5-1 in his six starts despite modest passing production.

Hundley believes Murray also entered a much different situation than the one he had as the No. 1 overall pick in Arizona.

"When you come in as the No. 1 pick, they will build the offense around you. Now you don't get that luxury. You come in and they say, 'We're running this. Do with it what you can. And if you can't succeed in this and lead us to where we want to go, we're going to find somebody else.' It's not that same pull when you come in as the No. 1 pick. So it's an interesting spot to be in."

Murray has also dealt with multiple injuries this season. He suffered a concussion in Minnesota's season opener before sustaining a hamstring strain following the Vikings' Week 8 loss to the Detroit Lions. O'Connell subsequently described Murray as "week to week," and the quarterback has not played since.

"I personally thought he was going to do a lot better when you look at the Vikings' past QB history of coming in and revamping their career. I said before the season I thought that would happen with Kyler, but I haven't seen what we're used to seeing with him," Hundley said.

Minnesota has since reunited with Kirk Cousins, acquiring him from the Las Vegas Raiders for a 2027 third-round pick. Cousins led the Vikings to a 30-10 victory over the Green Bay Packers in his first start back with the organization.

Cousins will make his second consecutive start when the Vikings (7-2) face the San Francisco 49ers (7-1) on Sunday night in Mexico City.

#2686. Brian Flores: Vikings plan to rotate players in order to deal with altitude in Mexico City

The 49ers are spending the week in Colorado in order to acclimate to the altitude at which their Week 11 game in Mexico City will be played. The Vikings are not.

But the Vikings have a plan for ensuring that their players are ready for the conditions.

“It’s a tough place to play,” Vikings defensive coordinator Brian Flores told reporters on Tuesday regarding the game in Mexico. “I think it’s certainly taxing, you know, I think you’re — I think as a staff, we’re already talking about, ‘Hey, we’re gonna have to rotate these guys and get, you know, everybody’s gonna have to be ready to play.’ And so, yeah, I mean, it’s certainly something that we’re going to have to deal with.

“But there’s always, you know, elements that we’re dealing with, whether it’s cold or rain or things like that. Obviously, we’re indoors, so we don’t deal with much of that. But that’s the environment we’re gonna be in. I think we prepare them to play in that environment, but I’m not gonna be silly and say, ‘Hey, everybody, hey, you’ve got to go in there for 12 straight plays.’ I mean, we’re not doing that.

“So we’ll have rotations, we’ll have a plan in place. Inevitably, we’re gonna have to fight through some of that. It’s not gonna be your average game where, you know, altitude, you just have — you literally have less oxygen. So we’ve got to do a great job as a staff of making sure — watching guys, and making sure we got guys in and out, you know, as much as we can.”

His broader message to the defense is clear: “We’re gonna need everybody.”

Regardless of the plan to rotate players, the question becomes whether the 49ers will be able to rely more heavily on their best players because they’re properly acclimated to the thinner air. The Vikings are choosing a different strategy, and it’s going to create an obvious apples to apples comparison between the approaches that the two teams have taken — especially if the 49ers players seem to be fresh and the Vikings players seem to be gassed.

#2685. Joel Embiid has huge night, but Sixers still have plenty of work to do

The Sixers' 135-123 win over the Brooklyn Nets was powered by Joel Embiid's best game of the season. The big fella dropped 37 points, shooting 13-of-22 from the field while getting to the free throw line 10 times. That included knocking down a pair of threes after only making one shot from behind the arc in his previous nine games.

Embiid built off his previous outing with continued aggression. He took 14 of his 22 shots in the paint, the consistent attack of the rim helped him get to the line so frequently. The difference, according to him, was being ready to attack from tip-off.

"It kind of feels like I've been going out there and letting myself go a bit," Embiid said, "so that was just the one adjustment we made, because I feel like we felt that impacted everything. The more force I play with, the more aggressive I am, the better the shot is going to be or is going to look."

In the walk off interview with NBC Sports Philly's Amy Fadool, Embiid said hitting a couple three balls felt good, especially because his shot's been "so broke."

It wasn't hard to see how that was translating into confidence for Embiid. For the second game in a row, he attempted a self-lob off the backboard to throw down a massive slam. Thankfully, for the second game in a row, the attempt got broken up before he could attempt the dunk. Despite the unsuccessful attempt, Embiid thinks that is a sign that he is playing the right way.

"I'm so used to playing hard that when I don't do that it affects everything else," he said. "I just wanted to go back to playing hard. That's the main thing. It's a long season, we're all gonna have these moments, so it's about being balanced."

Embiid's night was nearly overshadowed by the team blowing a 24-point lead to one of the worst teams in the league. They let the Nets shoot 55% in the second half as they cut that lead down to four points multiple times late.

What's held the Sixers back from their first true blowout win of the season has been the offense falling into place. Even after the 135-point performance, they're still 17th in the league in points scored per 100 possessions. As guys like Embiid start to find their footing, they do feel like their process is getting better.

"We're far from perfect, far from where we want to be," said VJ Edgecombe, "but I think we are trending in the right direction when it comes to offensively."

Jaylen Brown also seems to have found a groove over his past two games, scoring 31 points in each of those outings. He continues to preach that more time is what's going to allow him to find comfort with this group. As the chemistry develops on the floor, the camaraderie continues to show itself off the floor. Brown happened to drop a "trust the process" when detailing his acclimation right when Embiid was walking by, something the big man took great pleasure in.

The night was also nearly overshadowed by a couple injury scares for Brown. He had a scary-looking collision with Julius Randle with a cut on his head, and checked out of the game with 1:44 left as he felt some hamstring tightness.

Brown said he'll have to see how he feels in the morning, but wasn't too concerned about it compared to hamstring injuries he's dealt with in the past. As for the collision with Randle, the cut made him have to wear a headband, which led to more joking around from his teammates.

Edgecombe gave him some grief for donning a headband, something Brown said he has never done before in his career but a look Edgecombe sports quite often.

"I tell him he don't even know how to swag out the headband, he just looked nasty," Edgecombe said, "but you gotta protect, gotta cover up that little boo-boo."

As teammates continued to give him a hard time over the headband, Brown obliged his teammates by hitting some ninja poses. He joked that it might be something he needs to lean into even more.

"It might be a thing going forward," Brown said. "I do martial arts and shit, maybe it's time."

On a serious note, the Sixers know they still haven't picked up a dominant, blowout win on the season, despite having that chance in Brooklyn. As their stars start to figure things out offensively, they do feel like it's only a matter of time.

"We're putting together a bunch of games where we're winning. So I just think when I say we haven't even had a like a dominant win yet that you know we're capable of," Edgecombe said, "but that's where we got to continue to just keep getting better."

The good news for the Sixers is they've got themselves at the top of the Eastern Conference while still searching for that.

#2683. Nvidia has lost $2 trillion in value. Here's what options traders expect from earnings

Jensen Huang's forced exit from Nvidia shocked Wall Street and Washington, wiped $2 trillion off its market value, and raised serious questions about national security and regulation of the AI complex. Traders, for the moment, must content themselves with the comparatively mundane business of Tuesday's earnings, and options traders are expecting big moves.

With volatility in the stock near the highest since April 2025, options traders are now pricing a 7% reaction in the stock after its report, the biggest implied move going into an Nvidia report since February 2025, and well above the median move of 3.2% over the last eight quarters.

Implied volatility reached 68 Monday, compared to under 40 in early October, according to ThinkOrSwim data. Nvidia vol reached a multi-year high above 92 in the aftermath of President Trump's tariff Liberation day in April '25. In other words, the company that for much of the AI boom was the most reliable winner in the market, is now more volatile than all but 35 companies in the S&P 500.

"Longer term, I see significant downside risk unless Nvidia can attract an exceptional leader, someone like Tim Cook or AMD's Lisa Su," Nigam Arora, founder of the Arora report, said in a text ahead of the company's after-market report on Tuesday.

Since hitting all-time highs in October, Nvidia shares have fallen 35%, shedding $2 trillion in market capitalization along the way. Including Nvidia, there are only five companies in the S&P 500 worth more than $2 trillion. However, there is evidence that bulls have been holding out hope while bears snatch up profits: the ratio of open puts to call contracts has actually widened towards calls, according to Barchart data. The ratio is currently 0.87, down from a one-year high of 1.02 two weeks ago and the lowest since the first week of October.

The most popular contract by volume on Monday was the 155-strike put expiring at the bell Monday, but the most popular trade for later expiry was the 160-strike call expiring Thursday, and the second-most-common purchase was the 190-strike call expiring Friday, SpotGamma data show.

One catch is that most call contracts traded Monday looked like neutral to bearish positions opened by traders selling calls. Of the 5 million options traded on Monday, 3 million were calls, but more were likely opened by sellers than buyers. Of the $2.3 billion in options premium traded on Nvidia Monday, $1.2 billion was tied to puts, SpotGamma data show.

With bears taking profits in puts the past month, there's another potential snag on open interest. There are very few open put contracts at or below the $157 level at which the stock closed Monday. That suggests any bad surprise would find many traders with little downside protection.

By the same token, there's notable open interest in call contracts from the 175-strike and above, suggesting profit-taking on a bounce could limit Nvidia's recovery past levels it breached over the past week.

Either way, Tuesday's earnings could provide fireworks.

#2678. Packers players will get their mandatory four days off after Week 12 game

The Packers have a bye in Week 11. Under the Collective Bargaining Agreement, the players are entitled to four consecutive days off.

Coach Matt LaFleur told reporters on Monday that, after discussions with the NFL Players Association, the Packers have decided to give their four days off following the first game after the bye.

It makes sense; there are fewer total days between the Packers’ Week 10 game and their Week 12 game (10) than there are between the Week 12 game and the Week 13 game (11).

The situation flows directly from the decision to play a Wednesday game, on the night before Thanksgiving. It removes the traditional two-week break, cobbling together a pair of mini-byes.

For the players, the break likely will coincide with Thanksgiving weekend, possibly giving them Thursday, Friday, Saturday, and Sunday off.

The Rams, in contrast, will surely be taking their four-day break before the Week 12 Wednesday night game. They play their Week 13 game against the Chiefs only eight days later, on the following Thursday night.

#2677. Dodge’s NASCAR Return Gets Its First Crew Chief

Dodge is finally coming back to the NASCAR Cup Series in 2027, and the team has picked its first crew chief. Kaulig Racing named Aaron Kramer to lead Ty Dillon's car for the upcoming manufacturer return.

Kramer has a specific skill set that fits the long-term plan. He is 38 years old and hails from Wellsville, New York. Before this job, he served as Cole Custer's crew chief at the Haas Factory team during the 2025 and 2026 seasons. His background includes years in engineering roles at RFK Racing. There, he worked as Chris Buescher's lead race engineer when Buescher won five Cup Series races between 2022 and 2024. He also worked with Ryan Newman during his time there.

The new hire is a UNC-Charlotte mechanical engineering graduate who started his career in 2010. Kaulig Racing CEO Chris Rice highlighted the need for this specific background in a statement. "Aaron has the exact engineering mindset and practical background we need as we lay the groundwork for 2027," Rice said, according to AOL.com. The team is hiring a driver coach and building a technical foundation from scratch.

Kramer himself sees the job as a major career step. He noted that his resume includes car preparation, vehicle performance, and race engineering. "Stepping into this role with Kaulig Racing at such a big turning point for the team is a great opportunity," Kramer said in a prepared statement. Solid engineering, relentless preparation, and immense focus are required to launch a Cup program from scratch with a returning manufacturer like Dodge, he noted.

The team faces a heavy workload in the months ahead. The No. 10 and No. 16 Dodges are being tested and developed on track by Ty Dillon alongside veteran A.J. Allmendinger. This is the second straight year Kaulig has faced a massive development task. Marking the OEM's comeback to NASCAR competition following a 13-year absence, the team ran five Ram trucks in the Craftsman Truck Series in 2026.

#2675. JJ Redick is encouraging the Lakers to foul more

Embracing contact and physicality is paramount to being a good-to-great defensive unit. The Los Angeles Lakers intend to become a good-to-great defensive unit, so they can properly complement their league-leading offense and compete for a title this season.

Currently, they're middling and sit 15th in defensive rating, including 11th over their eight-game winning streak, per Cleaning the Glass. Head coach JJ Redick wants his players to assume a hard-nosed identity, even to the point of fouling — at least in certain contexts.

"We encourage physicality. We encourage – if we're not in the bonus and somebody goes to follow and they go to set the on-ball screen to ghost or touch roll, just two-hand shove them…We encourage that," Redick told reporters prior to Sunday's win over the Phoenix Suns. "The biggest thing for us is being smart about when we're fouling and right now we're giving up 6.1 free throws per game on non-shooting fouls in the bonus and that's entirely too many. I think that's bottom third in the NBA right now so we're fouling too much in the bonus. But if we're not in the bonus, foul. That's good. That part doesn't bother me. Boxing out, whatever it may be, our physical players need to be physical and we're not going to tell them to play with handcuffs on."

The Lakers' propensity for putting opposing teams on the line with non-shooting fouls once they're in the bonus has hamstrung their defense. According to Cleaning the Glass, they're conceding a .247 free-throw rate to opponents, the 24th-worst mark in the league. During Redick's first two seasons as head coach of the Lakers, they touted one of the 10 lowest opposing free-throw rates both years.

Perhaps, that increased mark is the byproduct of Los Angeles generating an NBA-high 18.7% opponent turnover rate after finishing 15th in 2025-26 and 18th in 2024-25. Each of the top five in opponent turnover rate sports a bottom 10 opponent free-throw rate this season. Last year, three of the top five did. In 2024-25, six of the top seven were 20th or worse.

Moving forward, the Lakers will have to better balance these realities to fulfill Redick's wishes and build a physical-yet-discerning defense.

After Robinson compiled the only 1,000-yard season of his four-year career with the Giants last year, catching 92 passes for 1,014 yards, the Titans gave the 5-foot-8, 185-pounder a four-year, $70 million contract with $38 million guaranteed.

Robinson has caught just 38 passes for 272 yards for the Titans. He is averaging just 4.2 catches per game after averaging 5.5 and 5.8 per game the last two seasons with the Giants. Robinson's 7.2 yards per catch is also a career low.

#2673. Belief and bench help NI scale new heights

After going 500 minutes without conceding a goal, Northern Ireland could be forgiven for having to make adjustments to play from behind.

Having travelled to Budapest knowing a point against Hungary would be enough to top Nations League B2, Barnabas Varga's strike after 35 minutes for the hosts at the Puskas Arena altered the picture and, for the first time in the campaign, left Michael O'Neill's side having to chase a game.

Their response to the deficit - on the pitch, from the bench and high in the stands where their suspended manager was watching the game - displayed the best characteristics of a side who had not won at this level before this unbeaten campaign.

While the point from a 1-1 draw in Budapest will be long remembered for Justin Devenny's brilliant equaliser and the scenes of celebration that greeted promotion to the competition's top tier for the first time, it was beyond the highlights and headlines that O'Neill saw the emerging qualities of his side.

"We went behind tonight and it was difficult, you know? Big stadium, big atmosphere, but we came out and played well in the second half and got what we needed from the game," said the manager.

"Those are things that I'm very pleased that are in this squad at such a young age. The technical side of the game we'll always look to improve on, but in terms of the physical, the tactical and the mental side of a game, we're very far ahead of where I thought we would be at this time."

Northern Ireland draw with Hungary to seal Nations League promotion

Watch: Devenny the hero as NI earn promotion

Hungary enjoyed more than 70% possession in the first half, and they could have been further than a goal ahead with Liverpool star Dominik Szoboszlai striking the base of the post after only five minutes.

O'Neill made his first change at half-time, bringing on veteran striker Josh Magennis, while Kieran Morrison, Brodie Spencer and Paul Smyth all followed in the second half.

Magennis and Morrison made a real impact before the equaliser, but it was the belief shown by all involved that O'Neill felt was key to the turnaround.

"I felt in the first half from where I was seeing the game, we just looked a little bit leggy which is natural," added O'Neill.

"I just felt we needed that freshness at the right points in the game that helped us, but it was the belief that we could get back into the game, and the belief that we could see the game out.

"I think we showed a lot of attributes that are in this squad at this moment in time and certainly in the second half that was that was the case."

For all the plaudits earned by this team who conceded just one goal in six games while picking up 12 points, among the most impressive aspects of the campaign has been who they have had to do without.

Talismanic Liverpool defender Conor Bradley has not played for this side in over 12 months through injury.

Ali McCann is another who missed both 2026 Nations League windows, while influential defender Daniel Ballard featured only in the first game in Georgia before a hamstring injury.

Arsenal's Ceadach O'Neill was forced to pull out of the November squad through injury at a time when he would perhaps have been the most natural replacement for Jamie Donley after the striker picked up a second booking against Ukraine last week and had to miss the finale through suspension.

Having taken over an ageing squad four years ago, O'Neill has rebuilt his playing panel with a group that feels as if it is getting deeper with each international window.

"The encouraging thing is we have players on the outside - we've Bradley to come back in, Ballard to come back in, Ali McCann, young Ceadach, Eoin Toal," added the former Stoke City and Blackburn Rovers boss.

"We have good competition for places now. We're going to need that competition, and some players are going to miss out, but we are building depth to the squad that will help us going forward."

Come the next Nations League, that will be all the way into the uncharted territory of the top tier.

He stuck with the cadence there, got the free play, and I thought [he] delivered an excellent ball to our receiver. I know our receiver can make that play.

“When you just look at his element and what he had to do, like I said yesterday, he did everything we asked him to do, and I am really encouraged by it.”

“I think we all probably could notice that, right? That wasn’t shocking at all. It’s just like I’ve kind of said with him, you can meet all you want, and you can watch all the film, [but] he keeps it very simple. At least that’s what it comes across as.

“It’s just going to be getting as many reps as he can. That’s what stinks about yesterday [was] only having 34 reps and trying to get a little bit of a run game going, which we obviously didn’t. Not a huge sample size as of yesterday, but hey, have another one this week, and we’ll take it from there.”

#2667. Gators Stay Perfect After Strong Performance in Players Era Opener

The Florida Gators continued their impressive start to the 2026-27 college basketball season Tuesday, defeating Notre Dame 86-78 in the opening round of the Players Era 8 tournament in Las Vegas. The No. 1 ranked Gators entered the matchup undefeated and faced one of their toughest tests of the young season against a Fighting Irish team that also had not lost.

Florida improved to 5-0 with the victory, while Notre Dame suffered its first defeat and dropped to 4-1. The Gators showed why they are considered one of the nation's top teams, using a strong first half and timely plays down the stretch to secure the win.

Florida established control during the opening half, putting together an impressive offensive performance. The Gators attacked the basket, moved the ball effectively and found ways to create opportunities against Notre Dame's defense.

Their efforts resulted in a 47-32 halftime advantage. Florida's ability to build a 15-point lead gave the team valuable breathing room heading into the second half.

Alex Condon and Thomas Haugh helped establish the offensive rhythm, while the Gators received important contributions from their supporting cast. Urban Klavzar also provided a major scoring boost, giving Florida another offensive weapon that Notre Dame struggled to contain.

The Fighting Irish refused to go away quietly. After struggling to keep pace during the first half, Notre Dame responded with a much better offensive performance after halftime.

The Irish scored 46 points in the second half, cutting into Florida's advantage and making the closing minutes more competitive. Their improved shooting helped create pressure, forcing the Gators to continue executing instead of simply protecting their lead.

However, Florida had enough answers to prevent the comeback. Whenever Notre Dame threatened to make the game even closer, the Gators found important baskets and defensive stops to maintain control.

Florida's balanced offensive attack proved to be one of the biggest differences. Alex Condon finished with 19 points, 10 rebounds and five assists, delivering an excellent all-around performance. Haugh added 14 points and nine rebounds, continuing to show why he is such an important piece of Florida's frontcourt.

Klavzar led the Gators with 22 points, providing a major scoring contribution off the bench. Denzel Aberdeen also reached double figures with 13 points.

Notre Dame showed plenty of fight, particularly after halftime, but the early deficit ultimately proved too difficult to overcome.

The victory gives Florida another important win as it continues its pursuit of the Players Era 8 championship. With the tournament featuring several strong programs, the Gators will need to maintain their level of play throughout the week.

Florida's ability to win despite Notre Dame's second half push should give the team confidence moving forward. The Gators are now 5-0 and have another opportunity to demonstrate why they entered the season among the favorites to compete for a national championship.

#2666. For the Seahawks this year, Drew Lock's numbers blow Sam Darnold's away

When Seahawks coach Mike Macdonald confirmed that Sam Darnold will start in Week 12 against the 49ers, assuming he is medically cleared, it was no surprise: The Seahawks signed Darnold to a three-year, $100 million contract last year and won the Super Bowl with Darnold at the helm. Quarterbacks like that don’t often get benched.

But Drew Lock has made it reasonable to question whether Darnold should get benched.

That’s because Lock has played three full games, and most of a fourth, while Darnold has missed time with injuries this season. And Lock hasn’t just been better than Darnold. Lock has been so much better that there’s no comparison between the two.

The Seahawks are 4-0 in the games when Lock was their primary quarterback. They’re 2-4 in the games with Darnold as the primary quarterback.

Of the 37 NFL quarterbacks who have thrown at least 90 passes this season, Lock ranks first with a passer rating of 125.6. Darnold ranks 31st with a passer rating of 79.5.

Lock has still not thrown an interception this season. Darnold has thrown nine.

Lock has completed 73.1 percent of his passes. Darnold has completed 60.2 percent.

Lock is averaging 9.0 yards per pass. Darnold is averaging 7.0 yards per pass.

Lock has been sacked on 5.1 percent of his dropbacks. Darnold has been sacked on 7.5 percent of his dropbacks.

With seven games left in the season, the 6-4 Seahawks are going to have a battle to get back to the playoffs. Macdonald believes Darnold is the best quarterback for the job. The numbers call that into question.

#2665. Despite the loss, Rockets Alperen Sengun continues his career-best shooting

When the Houston Rockets traded for Alperen Sengun in the 2021 NBA draft, they knew they were getting one of the best international players. An MVP of the Basketball Super League at 19, Sengun was already dominating overseas professional leagues.

Over the years, Sengun has gone from an up-and-coming young star to one of the best players in the NBA and a two-time All-Star. Throughout his NBA career, Sengun has also been one of the best post players in the NBA. He has shown he can score in the paint against the NBA's best defenders. Even with his scoring ability in the post, his offensive game has at times been limited by struggles not only in the mid-range but also from the 3-point range.

NBA teams are smart; they have multiple scouts and entire departments dedicated to knowing every strength and weakness of their opponents. Opposing teams quickly realized that if Sengun gets to his spot in the paint, it's usually too late to stop him. So teams started sagging off Sengun on pick-and-rolls, and whenever he got the ball away from the basket, they forced him to take a shot from outside.

That defensive strategy made life a lot harder for Sengun at times, as it became every opposing team's strategy when facing the Rockets. Sengun shot just 24.8% from the 3-point range in his first season, on 1.6 attempts. The next season, Sengun shot a higher percentage at .333 but at half the volume of the first season, as he sometimes hesitated on wide-open 3-pointers, choosing to pass on them and drive into a crowded paint.

Sengun's 3-point shooting dropped over the next two seasons from .333 to a career-worst .233 in his fourth season. Sengun has often said that is the one part of his offense that needs to improve if he wants to unlock his entire game. Sengun worked tirelessly on his 3-point shooting every summer, every practice, and before every game. Sengun has talked for years about his confidence in shooting 3-pointers, as he told SB Nation three years ago during training camp.

The hard work is starting to pay off in his sixth season, as Sengun is off to the best 3-point shooting start of his career.

HOUSTON, TEXAS – APRIL 24: Alperen Sengun #28 of the Houston Rockets shoots a three pointer during the first quarter of the game against the Los Angeles Lakers at Toyota Center on April 24, 2026 in Houston, Texas. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Kenneth Richmond/Getty Images) | Getty Images

Sengun, now in his sixth season, is starting to translate all the hard work he puts into his 3-point shot into the game. Not only has Sengun improved his outside shooting, but he leads the Rockets in 3-point shooting at 48.1 percent. It is on only 2.1 attempts per game, but that is also a career-high for Sengun. A high volume of shots from downtown has never been Sengun's goal; it is more about him being a threat from long range, which opens up the court for the Rockets' center. It's not just that he is making 3-pointers; it's that he isn't hesitating from deep when teams play him for the drive.

Sengun has made a 3-pointer in a career-high six straight games and in eight of his last ten games. For Sengun, if he keeps improving from deep, teams will have to respect him from that range, which gives him more space; with his ability to handle the ball and his court vision, it could take his offensive game to an entirely different level.

With a reliable 3-point game and improved play on the defensive end, Sengun could be in serious contention for MVP votes at the end of the season. Pretty good for a player 16 other teams passed on in 2021.

#2663. Highs and lows of Fernando Mendoza’s starting debut for Las Vegas Raiders

Fernando Mendoza's first NFL start in the Las Vegas Raiders' Week 10 matchup against the Seattle Seahawks was a tale of two halves.

In the first half, he completed 11 of 13 passes for 90 yards, with both incompletions being throwaways, and had five rushes for 21 yards and a touchdown. According to Sumer Sports, that resulted in a total EPA of 6.56, an EPA per play of 0.328 and a 55 percent success rate — pretty good numbers. However, the second half was a different story, going six-for-16 with 53 passing yards and one rush for one yard to post a negative 13.49 total EPA, negative 0.642 EPA per play and a 14.3 percent success rate.

For the entire game, the No. 1 pick completed 17 of 29 passes (58.6 percent) for 143 passing yards, along with six rushes for 22 yards and a touchdown. That netted a negative 6.93 total EPA, negative 0.169 EPA per play and a 34.1 percent success rate.

For more context, Pro Football Focus credited him with a 66.7 percent adjusted completion percentage (accounting for the two throwaways and one drop), a slightly below-baseline (60.0) 59.8 passing grade and a slightly above-baseline rushing grade (65.2) to sit at a 62.2 overall mark.

While the numbers above help paint part of the picture, let's dive into the tape to see the full story.

To help get the rookie going, Klint Kubiak dialed up bootlegs on Mendoza's first three throws. The clip above is the third one and is a great decision by the quarterback.

His first read is to see if the go route from the outside receiver is available for a one-on-one opportunity. However, the Seahawks have that covered with the corner underneath and the boundary safety on top of the receiver to execute the Cover 2 side of the Cover 6 call.

Mendoza's next two options are either tight end Michael Mayer, who runs a chip release into a curl route, or wide receiver Jalen Nailor on a crossing/drag route. The MIKE linebacker is all over Mayer to take that away, and the WILL linebacker turns to ROBOT and help the corner against Nailor. Meanwhile, fullback Connor Heyward is working across the formation to sell the run fake and run a flat route.

Heyward gets contacted by the edge rusher, who ends up getting pressure on Mendoza. However, Mendoza does a good job of going through his reads, finding Heyward and throwing around the free rusher to dump it to the fullback, who picks up five on second and six to set up a third-and-short situation.

This isn't a sexy throw by any means, but it's a good process to put the offense in a favorable third down situation.

Here, we'll get a look at the touchdown run that got Allegiant Stadium rocking and is eerily similar to the run the Indiana product had to win the National Championship over Miami last January.

It's third and 10 as the Raiders come out in an empty formation where the Seahawks respond with a five-man box and both safeties deep pre-snap. That's the perfect look to run a quarterback draw into, and Mendoza sells it well by taking a drop with his first step and eyeing wide receivers Jalen Nailor and Tre Tucker post-snap, keeping the safeties in the end zone.

Then, he takes off and does a great job of setting up left guard Spencer Burford's block by getting downfield and pressing the five-yard line. That allows Burford to position himself between the linebacker and ball carrier. Finally, Mendoza shows off some impressive vision to see the field safety overpursue, cut back to the other hashmark and put the ball in the painted area.

If the previous clip was the No. 1 pick's best play of the game, the clip above is the best throw he made on the afternoon.

Seattle starts with a two-high look pre-snap before rotating the boundary safety into the hook-to-curl area to play Cover 3. That takes away the curl from Nailor on the curl-flat concept. Maybe Mendoza can hit Mayer on the flat route, but the linebacker manages to avoid the pick and stay on top of the route. So, that's a fine decision to hold onto the ball. But the problem is that the pocket starts to break down with Trey Zuhn III getting bull-rushed by Byron Murphy II.

Mendoza manages to get out of trouble, but he can settle down and pick his eyes up at the 10-yard line instead of continuing to scramble. That would have allowed him to find slot receiver Cody White earlier — who is wide open on the corner route with a coverage bust from Devon Witherspoon — and hit White with some room to run. Regardless, Mendoza eventually finds White and delivers a strike on the run for a 22-yard gain near the sideline.

This is where things started to get a little messy for the rookie, as the play above comes with about a minute left in the second quarter. Mendoza had a few missed opportunities for explosive plays in the game, with the one above being the biggest.

The Seahawks show a two-safety coverage pre-snap but rotate into Cover 3 with the boundary safety spinning down. Since the Raiders are in a three-by-one formation, the boundary corner is locked in man coverage against Tucker, the Silver and Black's best deep threat.

Tucker beats press coverage and has a couple of steps on the corner, while the field or deep safety isn't in a position to help the corner on a pass outside of the numbers. Mendoza gets to Tucker in his progression but doesn't pull the trigger. Maybe left guard Spencer Burford losing control of Murphy discouraged the quarterback from letting it rip, but he has enough time and a clean enough pocket to make that throw.

Instead, Mendoza opts to scramble and only picks up one yard. To be fair, this was the play that had the 4th and 1/first and 10 confusion (see the markers on the field and the scorebug on the top of the screen). So, maybe that played a factor in his decision, but this was certainly a missed opportunity.

Part of the reason Mendoza had such a low EPA is that he took six sacks on the afternoon. That was the result of some great play calling from Seattle's defensive mastermind/head coach Mike Macdonald, and the rookie quarterback's subpar feel for pressure.

This is a perfect play call by Macdonald. Vegas runs a play-action pass where the entire offensive line and the full back work left to sell the run fake, while running back Mike Washington Jr. inserts into the protection scheme on the right edge. Meanwhile, Seattle slants the defensive line to the strong side of the formation as the MIKE and WILL linebackers blitz, on top of the cornerback coming off the edge.

So, the Seahawks end up with a numbers advantage, where the Raiders have three blockers (Zuhn, right tackle DJ Glaze and Washington) to account for four pass-rushers (the edge, two linebackers and the corner). Also, Mendoza's back is turned to the defense, and Kubiak calls a shot play with two deep routes.

That being said, he gets turned around well before Witherspoon gets there and doesn't sense the pressure coming, failing to move off his spot. Again, this is a tough look — especially for a quarterback making his first start.

But if we back up to the pre-snap look, the safety playing outside the hashmark with the corner in press coverage was the tell that Witherspoon was blitzing, especially since the receiver is in a reduced or cut split (meaning between the numbers and hashmark). That creates a learning opportunity for the young QB.

Another sack that highlights how Macdonald tested Mendoza's ability to read the defense when the picture changes post-snap and handle pressure.

Seattle comes out in a double A-gap look with a seven-man front, where four pass-rush threats are on the weak side of the offensive line (in this instance, the offense's right side). So, the quarterback changes the protection, sliding it to the right. However, Macdonald doesn't run a blitz but rather a simulated pressure, where all four defenders on the right side drop into coverage, and the nickel cornerback rushes through the C-gap for a four-man rush.

Since the line is sliding right and there's an edge rusher outside of the tight end, left tackle Kolton Miller stays with Murphy in the protection call, and Mayer sticks with the edge rusher, leaving Witherspoon unblocked as a free rusher. Mendoza doesn't see or feel the pressure and ends up taking another big hit for a sack on third down.

To be fair, it looks like he was trying to throw hot to Ashton Jeanty. But Witherspoon closed so quickly that he had already started making the tackle by the time Jeanty got his head around. Still, this is another instance where Mendoza needs to expect pressure with the nickel as close to the line of scrimmage as he is pre-snap.

Finally, Mendoza had a few throws where his accuracy got away from him, and the one above really hurt.

On this play, it's 2nd and 10 with about 2:45 to play and the Raiders trailing by 11. Seattle keeps the coverage pretty vanilla (for Macdonald's standards, at least), playing Cover 6 with quarters to the wide side of the field near the goal line. Mendoza makes the right read since the safety steps inside while wide receiver Jack Bech runs an out route. That's open for a potential touchdown.

However, Mendoza doesn't finish the throw, so the pass sails on him and falls incomplete. Granted, the pocket is collapsing, but he had enough room to deliver an accurate pass and just missed the opportunity.

#2662. Andres Cantor says Mauricio Pochettino leaves USMNT 'in a good place' as the U.S. 'has an edge' over CONCACAF rivals

Andres Cantor says Mauricio Pochettino leaves USMNT 'in a good place' as the U.S. 'has an edge' over CONCACAF rivals originally appeared on The Sporting News. Add The Sporting News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Just four months into the 2030 FIFA World Cup cycle, Mauricio Pochettino is on his way to Manchester United, leaving his post as head coach of the USMNT to make a return to club football and the Premier League.

The move leaves U.S. Soccer needing to find his replacement, hoping to maintain the growth displayed under Pochettino and hoping to find someone to bridge the gaps that Pochettino himself was unable to fill.

Whoever the U.S. hires, they will be well-positioned to build on what Pochettino brought to the program, says legendary play-by-play broadcaster Andres Cantor, who has spent nearly 40 years calling USMNT games.

Cantor says that Pochettino leaves the program "in a very good place" and that the outgoing boss has set the USMNT up for success.

MORE:Was Mauricio Pochettino a success with the USMNT?

"He took over a team with complacent players from the previous coach who were used to being handled in a totally different way," Cantor told The Sporting News ahead of Pochettino's departure for Manchester United. "I think he leaves the team in a good place, I honestly think so."

The longtime broadcaster, who called Pochettino's final two matches for Telemundo, says that the incoming Man United boss departs U.S. Soccer having elevated the careers of many Americans for both club and country.

"I think he leaves players that are much better than when he found them," Cantor said, highlighting Sebastian Berhalter, Cavan Sullivan, and Julian Hall.

MORE:Pochettino believes USMNT experience made him a "complete coach"

While Cantor knows there are holes in Pochettino's resume during his USMNT tenure, he believes there are explainable reasons for why the Argentine walks away without winning any trophies or attaining any significant milestones while in charge.

"The detractors will say he leaves without any titles, which is true," Cantor said. "But the first Nations League that he took over he was barely a few months into the job and a few days into knowing the players.

"Then the Gold Cup, we know what happened with [Christian] Pulisic, he didn't want to come, and there were other players who were not in the mix either who weren't 100 percent or at their best. They made the final and lost a very tight game against Mexico.

"Then obviously the World Cup where I put a big asterisk before and after Belgium. I have no doubts after talking with Mauricio a couple of times, I came to the conclusion that the noise around the [Folarin] Balogun red card situation with Trump's involvement did affect the team as a whole. Obviously that was not the United States we had seen. Maybe they could have lost under normal circumstances, but I am pretty sure not that way."

According to Cantor, Pochettino leaves the USMNT as the clear kings of CONCACAF, ahead of their border rivals Mexico and Canada, and miles in front of the rest.

"I really think the U.S. has an edge," Cantor said when asked where the top three CONCACAF nations stack up against one another. "I think they have better overall players, they have a very good foundation now with the national team. I think they're better than Mexico, and I think they're better than Canada."

MORE:Pochettino's message to Pep Guardiola regarding the USMNT job: "Take it"

Now it's up to the federation to identify Pochettino's replacement. While Cantor would not say who he thought was the right choice — besides Pep Guardiola, who he says would be "a tremendous coup" to get — he did have one piece of advice for the federation.

"They are going from a top class coach in Mauricio Pochettino, so from a media standpoint, from an aura standpoint, they have to make the effort to go after somebody of his caliber on the world stage," Cantor says of the upcoming coaching search. "This team of players has proven itself that they can play at the highest level, so whoever the coach will be hopefully will continue at the pace of these players."

Alongside his duties calling games for U.S. Soccer, Cantor has signed on with Netflix to call games for the 2027 FIFA Women's World Cup, where the USWNT will hope to return to the top of the global game.

#2659. Dillon Brooks’ intensity remains essential to the Suns’ identity

There are plenty of things in this world that you love 85% of the time and can't stand the other 15%. Group texts immediately come to mind. You love being part of the conversation, whether it's with family members, lifelong friends, or your sports buddies discussing the latest developments surrounding the Phoenix Suns. But sometimes those conversations go completely off the rails, your phone starts vibrating like it's sitting on a washing machine during the spin cycle, and you find yourself eternally grateful for whoever developed the technology to mute group text notifications.

As it pertains to the Phoenix Suns, the same could be said about Dillon Brooks.

We were baptized in the Dillon Brooks experience a season ago, and the overwhelming majority of the returns were positive. He's a culture setter and an attitude definer, somebody whose presence dictates the level of physicality with which your basketball team operates. Last season, he also showed us that he has considerably more tools in his offensive arsenal than many of us anticipated, averaging a career-high 20.2 points on 17.1 field-goal attempts per game.

But with that tenacity comes an edge, and navigating it without going over can be a delicate exercise. That's where the other 15% of the Dillon Brooks experience resides. The technical fouls. The flagrant fouls. The moments when the very characteristics that make him such a valuable member of your basketball team become the source of your frustration. And oftentimes, it's the timing of those infractions that makes them particularly maddening. Late-game situations and high-leverage possessions are instances in which discipline needs to supersede emotion, and Brooks doesn't always recognize when that transition needs to occur.

You love what he brings to your basketball team 85% of the time. The other 15%? You find yourself wishing Dillon Brooks had the same mute button as your group texts.

That being said, we've seen a different version of Dillon Brooks thus far this season. He's averaging 13.8 points on 12.0 shot attempts per game. Maybe it starts with the haircut, but whatever has changed, it's manifesting itself in his performance on the basketball court.

With the acquisition of Miles Bridges, Brooks has looked somewhat out of place to begin the season (as has Bridges). Much akin to the duplicative nature of Devin Booker and Jalen Green's skill sets, the same can be said of Brooks and Bridges. They occupy similar spaces on the floor and are asked to perform similar functions within the starting unit. Be a quality point-of-attack defender, knock down the three ball when called upon, and provide some physicality and assistance on the glass.

The Suns have found themselves in an interesting conundrum as the season has unfolded. There are instances when neither player appears entirely certain of where he should be positioned, whom he should be covering, or how to navigate switches on the defensive end. That uncertainty creates a particularly frustrating defensive dynamic because it compromises the very aggression that makes both players effective.

When they're aggressive, miscommunication leaves somebody exposed, and the opposition makes them pay for it. And then there are possessions when it appears they're being overly cautious, almost hesitant to do anything for fear of doing it wrong. That too burns them defensively. For two players whose effectiveness is predicated in large part on their physicality and assertiveness, indecision can be debilitating. It's difficult to impose your will on a basketball game when you're still trying to figure out where you're supposed to be.

This conundrum has contributed to the challenges the starting unit has faced throughout the early stages of the season. They're not a horrible offensive unit, primarily because Devin Booker's consistency has served as the rising tide that lifts the boats around him. Defensively, however, this group has yet to establish an identity. And when you juxtapose that against what we're experiencing from the second unit, a group that clearly understands its individual roles and how those roles complement one another, it becomes glaringly apparent how discombobulated the starting five has been.

Last night against the Denver Nuggets, we saw head coach Jordan Ott make an adjustment that appeared to unlock Dillon Brooks. That adjustment? He opened the second quarter with Brooks alongside members of the second unit, and almost immediately we saw Dillon being Dillon again.

Through his first three minutes as part of that lineup, Brooks attempted four shots. Granted, he went 1-of-4 from the field, but what stood out to me was the decisiveness with which he was operating. There was a familiarity to his approach, an aggressiveness that we'd grown to appreciate a season ago. He looked empowered to assert himself offensively without having to navigate the overlapping responsibilities that exist within the starting five.

The shots weren't necessarily falling, but the mentality was there. And from that point forward, Brooks delivered one of his best performances of the young season.

Now, I'm not about to head to my group text and flood its participants with messages advocating for Dillon Brooks to take 17 shots a night again. As Brooks has shown us throughout his career, his inefficiency can be his downfall. He attempted more shots than any member of the Phoenix Suns last night, going 6-of-17 from the field to get his 15 points. Not exactly great.

What this team needs more of is an engaged Dillon Brooks. Somebody who's emotionally invested in the game, actively disrupting what the opposition is attempting to accomplish, and embracing the alpha mentality that has become synonymous with his brand of basketball.

Brooks is the emotional leader of the starting unit, much like Jordan Goodwin is the emotional leader of the second unit. When Brooks is passive and isn't asserting himself, it affects his individual performance and, by extension, the performance of those around him. His intensity has a trickle-down effect on his teammates, and when that intensity is absent, you can feel it throughout the lineup.

Again, it's a fine edge to navigate. But it's one Brooks has navigated throughout his NBA career. It's his X factor, the characteristic that makes him special and gives him value beyond his statistical production.

Hell, he even picked up a horseshit technical foul last night after knocking down a three-pointer and barking at the opposing bench. Apparently, only Dillon Brooks can earn a technical foul for doing something we routinely see across the NBA. As frustrating as the whistle was, it was another indication of how engaged he was in the game.

It was his third technical foul of the season through 13 games, meaning he's averaging one every 4.3 games. That's a slightly more disciplined pace than a season ago, when he averaged one every 3.3 games en route to accumulating a league-leading 17 technical fouls.

The ideal balance, in my eyes, is a version of Brooks who takes somewhere around 11 shots per game, assumes greater offensive responsibility when sharing the floor with the second unit, and remains the emotional leader of the starting five. Defensively, you want him to take charge, communicate, dictate physicality, and impose his will, rather than operate with uncertainty and defer to those around him.

There's no perfect formula for finding that balance, particularly for a player whose greatest strength can occasionally become his greatest weakness. The Suns need Brooks to understand when to turn the volume up and when to dial it back. But they also need him to remain the emotional heartbeat of that starting unit. His aggression, physicality, and willingness to get under somebody's skin are part of what makes him valuable.

Give me an engaged Dillon Brooks, and I'll live with having to mute the group text from time to time. Now do me a favor, send this article to one of your friends via group text.

The Colts listed both of their starting cornerbacks as out of practice on Tuesday.

It was the second straight day that Sauce Gardner (calf) and Charvarius Ward (knee) received that designation, although it was just an estimation as the Colts only held a walkthrough on Tuesday. Both corners took part in last Sunday's 24-22 win over the Dolphins.

Left tackle Bernhard Raimann (concussion) and right tackle Jalen Travis (knee) were also listed as out for the second day in a row. Travis did not play against the Dolphins while Raimann only played six snaps. The nature of the concussion protocol will work against Raimann being avaiable against the Texans on Thursday.

Offensive tackle Charlie Heck (personal), tight end Will Mallory (hamstring), and defensive tackle Jerry Tillery (calf) were also listed as non-participants. Wide receiver Anthony Gould (toe) and long snapper Luke Rhodes (hamstring were limited. Guard Dalton Tucker (shoulder) was a full participant.

#2649. Brent Venables discusses the impact of Kip Lewis, Owen Heinecke at OU

When the Oklahoma Sooners take the field on Saturday against the Texas A&M Aggies, it will be the last time several key players play in front of fans in Norman. Two of those players are linebackers, Kip Lewis and Owen Heinecke.

They lead the Sooners in tackles this year. Heinecke leads the team with 83 tackles and tackles for loss at 9.5, whereas Lewis is second with 50 tackles and three sacks. Both arrived in Norman in 2022 and have played a big part in turning the defense around. The Sooners were known as one of the worst defensive programs in college football over the past decade. They finished No. 122 in total defense in Brent Venables' first year as head coach. But due to the help of Lewis, Heinecke and several other key players, Oklahoma has become one of the best defensive programs in college football over the last three seasons.

Venables talked about the impact Lewis and Heinecke have had in their time at Oklahoma.

"We're in a much different place than we were four and a half years ago when they got here," Venables said. "We wouldn't be here if it weren't for guys like that and several other players prior to them. I think they recognize the responsibility that they have to kind of be every bit of good of leaders and ambassadors that other players have as well."

Lewis and Heinecke have been part of Oklahoma's defensive transformation, helping establish the physicality and consistency that have become defining traits of the unit under Venables. Their production this season reflects their importance, but their impact extends beyond the stat sheet. Both have helped set the standard for younger players and played a role in building the culture that has made the Sooners' defense one of the program's biggest strengths.

#2648. Dolphins DT Jordan Phillips will undergo season-ending biceps surgery

Dolphins defensive tackle Jordan Phillips injured a biceps in Sunday’s loss to the Colts.

Marcel Louis-Jacques of ESPN reports that Phillips will undergo season-ending surgery.

The Dolphins announced they have placed Phillips on injured reserve.

A Dolphins’ fifth-round pick in 2025, Phillips has played 60 percent of the Dolphins’ defensive snaps this season. He was expected to have an even bigger role after Zach Sieler’s season-ending biceps injury.

Defensive tackle Zeek Biggers was also lost for the season on Sunday, injuring the lateral meniscus in his knee.
