#2645. Titans put Wan'Dale Robinson on injured reserve

The Titans have been happy with quarterback Cam Ward’s recent play, but he’ll have to keep it going without his most frequent target in the passing game.

Wide receiver Wan’Dale Robinson has been placed on injured reserve. Robinson injured his ankle during last Sunday’s loss to the Jaguars.

Robinson had 38 catches for 272 yards and three touchdowns while being targeted 54 times this season. Rookie Carnell Tate has missed the team’s last two games with a shoulder injury, but head coach Robert Saleh said on Monday that they hope the first-round pick can return in Week 11.

The Titans filled Robinson’s roster spot by signing wide receiver Xavier Restrepo off of the practice squad. Restrepo was Ward’s college teammate and he had three catches for 31 yards as a temporary elevation in Week 10.

They also announced that they have signed wide receiver Jamari Thrash to their practice squad.

#2644. Hawks' CJ McCollum out at least 4 weeks with calf strain, putting rookie Kingston Flemings in the spotlight

Atlanta Hawks guard CJ McCollum has a left calf strain and will be sidelined for at least four weeks. The Hawks announced the injury update on Tuesday.

McCollum sustained the injury in Sunday's win over the Milwaukee Bucks. Imaging has since revealed the calf strain. He'll be reevaluated in four weeks, per the Hawks.

Through 14 games, McCollum is averaging 14.5 points, 3.9 assists, 3.1 rebounds and 1.1 steals per game while shooting 43.3% from the floor and 35.2% from 3. The Hawks enter Tuesday at 8-6, tied with the Miami Heat for the fourth-best record in the Eastern Conference.

The injury is a blow for the Hawks as they lose a key playmaker and their veteran voice on the court.

McCollum, 35, began the season as Atlanta's starting point guard. But he gave way to rookie Kingston Flemings in the starting unit, recently telling the NBA on Prime that he approached head coach Quin Snyder about making the lineup change.

"I didn't like the way we were playing as a unit, as a starting unit," McCollum said. "I think we were a little sluggish. I think we were lackluster from a ball movement standpoint.

"I think we were stagnant, and we have a lot of guys that can create and make plays. And I think for Kingston, he's a guy who gets downhill. He's got tremendous speed. He's learning and growing. And I think it gives him a chance to kind of develop, for us as a franchise and for us as an organization. …

"The way I play, I can get to it off the bench, I can get to it as a starter. I'm mature enough to be able to handle different roles, different minutes."

With McCollum sidelined, the keys to the offense will belong to Flemings, whom the Hawks selected with the No. 8 pick in June's draft out of Houston. In 12 games including five starts, Flemings is averaging 7.8 points, 4.1 assists, 2.5 rebounds and 0.7 steals in 21.5 minutes per game.

Flemings' shooting efficiency has stood out, especially for a rookie. He's shooting 51.4% from the field and 42.9% on 1.8 3-point attempts per game. He's done so in limited playing time as McCollum has continued to play heavy minutes off the bench.

Flemings has been day-to-day with back tightness, but was reportedly able to practice on Tuesday. He projects to have an opportunity to build on his success with increased volume and playing time in McCollum's absence.

The Hawks also announced Tuesday that second-year backup forward Asa Newell will miss at least four months with a plantar plate tear in his left great toe that required surgery. Newell was averaging 3.6 points and 2.6 rebounds in 11 minutes per game in five appearances this season.

#2642. No. 25 LSU adds RJ Luis Jr., who briefly was under contract with 2 NBA teams, to its roster

LSU added RJ Luis Jr., who briefly spent time under contract with two NBA teams, to its roster on Tuesday, a day before the 25th-ranked Tigers' scheduled game against Louisiana-Monroe.

The move comes after LSU President Wade Rousse released a statement saying the university intended to comply with a district court ruling in Baton Rouge that said the NCAA and Southeastern Conference could not declare Luis ineligible or punish LSU for allowing Luis to play.

"LSU is committed to compliance with NCAA and SEC rules, but it is equally committed to obeying the orders of Louisiana's courts," Rousse said.

Luis, the 2025 Big East player of the Year with St. John's, spent parts of the 2025 NBA offseason with the Utah Jazz and Boston Celtics. He now joins a team that has won its first four games and this week entered the AP Top 25 on the heels of victory on Saturday over then-No. 14 Gonzaga.

"LSU, by rostering RJ, did the right thing," Luis' lawyer, Tony Clayton, told The Associated Press, noting that Luis never played in a regular-season NBA game and that the NCAA routinely issues waivers allowing players from professional leagues overseas to play for college teams in the United States.

LSU has three such international players on its roster: centers Brice Dessert and Michael Ruzic and forward Saliou Niang.

"It would be fundamentally unfair to RJ Luis to not allow him to play college ball when you have so many similarly situated young men who are playing in college now," Clayton said.

State district judge Ronald R. Johnson in East Baton Rouge Parish issued an injunction against the NCAA in Luis' case on Aug. 24. But the SEC shortly afterward strengthened rules against athletes who'd signed NFL, NBA or WNBA contracts, adding enforcement provisions that included fines of up to 50% of a program's revenue and half-season suspensions for coaches.null

Johnson subsequently ruled that the SEC could not punish LSU for allowing Luis to play, and that LSU could not cite SEC rules as a reason for keeping Luis off its roster.

In his statement, Rousse noted that Luis "secured his eligibility through a court order before the new SEC policy interpretation was announced. LSU supports the updated policy going forward, and we remain a proud and committed member of the SEC."

Rousse also noted that the SEC had confirmed to LSU that it would abide by the state court rulings in Luis' case.

LSU coach Will Wade, now in his second stint with the university that fired him in 2022 over NCAA recruiting violations, initially held off on placing Luis on the roster while the district court ruling was challenged in state appeals courts.

Last Friday, the Louisiana Supreme Court upheld Johnson's preliminary injunction against the NCAA, the SEC and LSU.

Whether Luis will remain on the roster for the entire season is unclear. That depends on how aggressively the NCAA and SEC continue to pursue the case, and the court's timeline.

Clayton, who declared after a previous favorable ruling in an eligibility case that justice was served to SEC Commissioner Greg Sankey "like a cold cup of gumbo," said he hopes the Luis matter can be settled amicably now.

"I would love to sit down with Sankey and buy him a hot cup of gumbo," Clayton said. "We've got to move forward. We've got to put it behind us. I just hope they allow this kid to live his life."

#2641. Texans CB Kamari Lassiter downgraded to a non-participant Tuesday

The Texans' injury report on Tuesday had only one change from the estimated report on Monday.

Cornerback Kamari Lassiter (wrist) did not practice after being listed as a full participant a day earlier.

Lassiter has played all but three snaps this season, playing 2,361 plays this season.

The Texans listed wide receiver Marvin Harrison Jr. (hamstring), defensive end Danielle Hunter (hamstring), tight end Brevin Jordan (foot/illness) and running back Woody Marks (concussion) as non-participants again.

Linebacker Jake Hummel (groin) remained limited.

Running back David Montgomery (groin), safety Reed Blankenship (hand), wide receiver Tank Dell (hand/shoulder), offensive tackle Blake Fisher (thumb), offensive guard Febechi Nwaiwu (knee), offensive lineman Keylan Rutledge (shoulder), cornerback Jaylin Smith (hamstring) and offensive guard Wyatt Teller (back) were full participants a second consecutive day.

Montgomery is on track to return after missing the past four games.
